  727:     if source is None and settings.input_path is None:
  728:         # ``input_path`` is None only because the caller stated this job came
  729:         # from no file, and no rows arrived either - so there is nothing to
  730:         # convert and nothing to read. Refused rather than defaulted: a
  731:         # placeholder path here would send pnezd.read at a file nobody named
  732:         # (docs/DESIGN.md section 1, amendment #26).
  733:         raise ValueError(
  734:             "This job has no input file and no parsed rows, so there is "
  735:             "nothing to convert. Either give JobSettings an input_path, or "
  736:             "hand run() a source - michspc.fileio.pnezd.parse_typed_point "
  737:             "builds one from a single typed coordinate."
  738:         )
  739: 
  740:     parsed = source or pnezd.read(settings.input_path)
  741: 
  742:     if settings.geoid_model is not None and not isinstance(
  743:         settings.geoid_model, geoid.GeoidModel
  744:     ):
  745:         # The #11-finding-1 class, at this field's likeliest call site: the
  746:         # argument replaced ``apply_geoid: bool``, so ``geoid_model=True`` is
  747:         # the exact habit a caller carries forward - and truthiness would
  748:         # accept it here, then fail attribute-by-attribute somewhere inside
  749:         # the loader. if/raise, never assert (-O strips asserts).
  750:         raise TypeError(
  751:             f"JobSettings.geoid_model must be a michspc.fileio.geoid."
  752:             f"GeoidModel record, or None to state that no geoid is applied; "
  753:             f"got {type(settings.geoid_model).__name__} "
  754:             f"({settings.geoid_model!r}). In particular True is not 'the "
  755:             f"default model' - that was apply_geoid's contract, retired by "
  756:             f"WP-V5. Pass geoid.GEOID18_MODEL, geoid.GEOID12B_MODEL, or a "
  757:             f"record from geoid.geoid_model_by_name()."
  758:         )
  759: 
  760:     if (
  761:         settings.geoid_model is not None
  762:         and settings.geoid_model not in geoid.ALL_GEOID_MODELS
  763:     ):
  764:         # Registry membership, checked BEFORE any point converts. The loaders
  765:         # deliberately accept a hand-built record (the suite exercises
  766:         # tampered tiles through one), but a JOB may not: report.py resolves
  767:         # the record back from the registry by name to cite the tile and its
  768:         # digest, so a non-registry record would convert every point and then
  769:         # fail with a bare KeyError at the record write - a whole conversion
  770:         # discarded at the last step, found by the WP-V5 review gate (LOW 1).
  771:         # Membership is by equality, so a caller who rebuilt a record with a
  772:         # registry model's exact facts is accepted: identical facts ARE the
  773:         # model. What is refused is a record whose facts the registry does
  774:         # not hold - a model this program cannot cite.
  775:         known = ", ".join(model.name for model in geoid.ALL_GEOID_MODELS)
  776:         raise ValueError(
  777:             f"JobSettings.geoid_model {settings.geoid_model.name!r} is not a "
  778:             f"registered geoid model, so the job record could not cite its "
  779:             f"tile and checksum. A job converts only against the models this "
  780:             f"program ships: {known}. Use the records in "
  781:             f"michspc.fileio.geoid, or geoid_model_by_name()."
  782:         )
  783: 
  784:     if settings.source_geoid_model is not None and not isinstance(
  785:         settings.source_geoid_model, geoid.GeoidModel
  786:     ):
  787:         # The impostor guard ``geoid_model`` carries above, for the input
  788:         # side: the two fields are chosen by parallel dropdowns, so the same
  789:         # habits reach both. if/raise, never assert (-O strips asserts).
  790:         raise TypeError(
  791:             f"JobSettings.source_geoid_model must be a michspc.fileio.geoid."
  792:             f"GeoidModel record, or None to state that no input-side geoid "
  793:             f"model is stated; got "
  794:             f"{type(settings.source_geoid_model).__name__} "
  795:             f"({settings.source_geoid_model!r}). Pass geoid.GEOID18_MODEL, "
  796:             f"geoid.GEOID12B_MODEL, or a record from "
  797:             f"geoid.geoid_model_by_name()."
  798:         )
  799: 
  800:     if (
  801:         settings.source_geoid_model is not None
  802:         and settings.source_geoid_model not in geoid.ALL_GEOID_MODELS
  803:     ):
  804:         # Registry membership, the geoid_model rule above verbatim: the job
  805:         # record cites the input model's tile and digest from the registry,
  806:         # so a model the registry does not hold cannot be documented.
  807:         known = ", ".join(model.name for model in geoid.ALL_GEOID_MODELS)
  808:         raise ValueError(
  809:             f"JobSettings.source_geoid_model "
  810:             f"{settings.source_geoid_model.name!r} is not a registered geoid "
  811:             f"model, so the job record could not cite its tile and checksum. "
  812:             f"A job converts only against the models this program ships: "
  813:             f"{known}. Use the records in michspc.fileio.geoid, or "
  814:             f"geoid_model_by_name()."
  815:         )
  816: 
  817:     if transformation is None and settings.source_geoid_model is not None:
  818:         # A horizontal job asked no vertical question, so an input-side
  819:         # geoid model answers nothing - the mirror of the vertical-datum
  820:         # refusal, arriving at the new field. Silently ignoring it would let
  821:         # a caller believe the input elevations' geoid statement was used.
  822:         raise ValueError(
  823:             f"This job is horizontal-only, but source_geoid_model "
  824:             f"({settings.source_geoid_model.name}) was supplied. An "
  825:             f"input-side geoid model exists to convert elevations between "
  826:             f"geoid models, and a horizontal job converts no elevation at "
  827:             f"all. Set vertical_mode to a mode that converts elevations, or "
  828:             f"state None; a horizontal job's factors come from geoid_model "
  829:             f"alone."
  830:         )
  831: 
  832:     if (
  833:         transformation is not None
  834:         and not transformation.is_identity
  835:         and settings.geoid_model is not None
  836:         and settings.source_geoid_model is not None
  837:     ):
  838:         # The compound-job refusal: a modeled datum shift AND a geoid change
  839:         # inside one Z column would stack two modeled corrections with no
  840:         # surface stating either alone. The honest form is two jobs, each
  841:         # with its own record - and the refusal says so rather than picking
  842:         # an order of application nobody stated. Guarded before the
  843:         # per-side era checks below, because the advice here (split the job)
  844:         # is the one that survives whichever era pairing arrives with it -
  845:         # today only NAVD 88 has published models, so this fires ahead of a
  846:         # NAPGD2022-era registry rather than after it.
  847:         raise ValueError(
  848:             f"This job states a geoid model on BOTH sides "
  849:             f"(source_geoid_model={settings.source_geoid_model.name}, "
  850:             f"geoid_model={settings.geoid_model.name}) of a modeled vertical "
  851:             f"transformation ({transformation.source.code} -> "
  852:             f"{transformation.target.code}). That would compound a modeled "
  853:             f"datum shift with a modeled geoid change inside one elevation, "
  854:             f"and no output could say which correction moved the height. "
  855:             f"Run two jobs: convert between the geoid models in an identity "
  856:             f"job ({transformation.source.code} -> "
  857:             f"{transformation.source.code}), then convert the datum with "
  858:             f"one geoid model stated."
  859:         )
  860: 
  861:     if transformation is not None and settings.source_geoid_model is not None:
  862:         # The per-side form of DESIGN.md #32's two-eras rule: the INPUT
  863:         # model's separations must be published for the INPUT elevations'
  864:         # own datum, because H_in + N_in is the ellipsoid height only when
  865:         # the two share an era. The output side keeps the guard below.
  866:         model = settings.source_geoid_model
  867:         if model.vertical_datum.code != settings.source_vertical_datum.code:
  868:             raise geoid.GeoidError(
  869:                 f"The {model.name} geoid model publishes separations for "
  870:                 f"heights in {model.vertical_datum.code}, and this job's "
  871:                 f"INPUT elevations are "
  872:                 f"{settings.source_vertical_datum.code}, so there is no "
  873:                 f"input height its separations can honestly combine with - "
  874:                 f"an ellipsoid height built from the two would mix eras "
  875:                 f"inside one number (DESIGN.md #32). State an input-side "
  876:                 f"model published for "
  877:                 f"{settings.source_vertical_datum.code} heights, or none."
  878:             )
  879: 
  880:     if transformation is not None and settings.geoid_model is not None:
  881:         # Plan section 3.5's fourth refusal, WIDENED at the WP-V6 review gate
  882:         # (DESIGN.md #41, superseding the plan's target-datum-only rule): the
  883:         # geoid model's datum must match EITHER endpoint of the vertical
  884:         # conversion, because the factors are computed from the height in the
  885:         # model's own era - the shifted height when the target matches, the
  886:         # source height when the source does (_convert_row). The plan's rule
  887:         # would have refused NAVD88 -> NGVD29 outright, dead-ending WP-V8's
  888:         # dropdowns for every NGVD 29 target with advice no interface offers;
  889:         # under this rule both modeled directions carry a NAVD 88 leg and
  890:         # work with both shipped models, and the era mixing #32 forbids never
  891:         # happens. What still refuses: a pair whose endpoints BOTH differ
  892:         # from the model's datum - today, only an NGVD29 -> NGVD29 identity
  893:         # job with a geoid model attached, where no NAVD 88 height exists at
  894:         # any stage. After the job's own geoid_model guards above, so a geoid
  895:         # impostor is still refused by the job's own message first.
  896:         model = settings.geoid_model
  897:         endpoint_codes = {
  898:             settings.source_vertical_datum.code,
  899:             settings.target_vertical_datum.code,
  900:         }
  901:         if model.vertical_datum.code not in endpoint_codes:
  902:             raise geoid.GeoidError(
  903:                 f"The {model.name} geoid model publishes separations for "
  904:                 f"heights in {model.vertical_datum.code}, but no stage of "
  905:                 f"this job's elevations is in that datum "
  906:                 f"({settings.source_vertical_datum.code} -> "
  907:                 f"{settings.target_vertical_datum.code}), so there is no "
  908:                 f"height the model's separations can honestly combine with - "
  909:                 f"an elevation factor built from the two would mix eras "
  910:                 f"inside one number (DESIGN.md #32). Run this job in "
  911:                 f"horizontal mode, which converts the coordinates and "
  912:                 f"carries the elevation through unchanged, or convert the "
  913:                 f"elevations to {model.vertical_datum.code} in a job that "
  914:                 f"targets it."
  915:             )
  916: 
  917:     # The factors' grid: the one side whose height and separation share an
  918:     # era (factors_geoid_model - output side preferred, else input side).
  919:     # For every pre-existing call shape this is settings.geoid_model's own
  920:     # grid, bit for bit: the #41-era NAVD88 -> NGVD29 shape normalizes its
  921:     # model to the input side and this reads the same record back.
  922:     factors_model = factors_geoid_model(settings, transformation)
  923:     grid = (
  924:         geoid.default_grid(factors_model) if factors_model is not None else None
  925:     )
  926: 
  927:     # The geoid-to-geoid conversion's two grids, loaded once per job exactly
  928:     # as the factors grid above is. None on every job that is not a swap -
  929:     # including the same model stated on both sides, which is the ordinary
  930:     # identity job with no swap step.
  931:     swap_models = geoid_swap_models(settings, transformation)
  932:     swap_grids = (
  933:         _GeoidSwapGrids(
  934:             source_model=swap_models[0],
  935:             target_model=swap_models[1],
  936:             source_grid=geoid.default_grid(swap_models[0]),
  937:             target_grid=geoid.default_grid(swap_models[1]),
  938:         )
  939:         if swap_models is not None
  940:         else None
  941:     )
  942: 
  943:     # The VERTCON pair, loaded ONCE per job exactly as the geoid grid above is
  944:     # - not per row (two 2.4 MB files re-read per point), and NOT for an
  945:     # identity: an identity applies no grid, apply_shift refuses a grid value
  946:     # for one, and a NAVD 88 to NAVD 88 job must succeed with the VERTCON
  947:     # files absent entirely.
  948:     vertcon_grids = (
  949:         vertcon.default_grids()
  950:         if transformation is not None and not transformation.is_identity
  951:         else None
  952:     )
  953: 
  954:     points: list[ConvertedPoint] = []
  955:     for row in parsed.rows:
  956:         points.append(
  957:             _convert_row(
  958:                 row, settings, grid, transformation, vertcon_grids, swap_grids
  959:             )
  960:         )
  961: 
  962:     return JobResult(
  963:         settings=settings,
  964:         points=tuple(points),
  965:         # The digest of the bytes the parser consumed, and nothing else. None
  966:         # when the rows arrived already parsed - see JobResult.input_sha256.
  967:         input_sha256=parsed.sha256,
  968:         input_row_count=len(parsed.rows),
  969:         skipped_blank_lines=parsed.skipped_blank_lines,
  970:         # The NAME of the model the factors were actually computed from, not
  971:         # the record: JobResult's contract predates the registry and
  972:         # report.py resolves the record back through geoid_model_by_name.
  973:         # For every pre-existing call shape this is settings.geoid_model's
  974:         # own name; since the per-side feature it is the factors side's, so
  975:         # a job whose only model is input-side (geoid_model=None,
  976:         # source_geoid_model=GEOID18) names the grid its factors really
  977:         # came from rather than claiming no geoid was applied.
  978:         geoid_model=(
  979:             factors_model.name if factors_model is not None else None
  980:         ),
  981:     )
  982: 
  983: 
  984: @dataclass(frozen=True)
  985: class _GeoidSwapGrids:
  986:     """A swap job's two models with their loaded grids - run()'s per-job
  987:     wiring, handed to ``_convert_row`` so the tiles are read once per job,
  988:     not per row."""
  989: 
  990:     source_model: geoid.GeoidModel
  991:     target_model: geoid.GeoidModel
  992:     source_grid: object
  993:     target_grid: object
  994: 
  995: 
  996: def _require_vertical_settings(
  997:     settings: JobSettings,
  998: ) -> VerticalTransformation | None:
  999:     """The vertical transformation this job will apply per point, or None.
 1000: 
 1001:     Every settings-level vertical refusal, in one place, before any point
 1002:     converts (docs/PLAN-vertical-datums.md section 3.5):
 1003: 
 1004:     * a vertical job missing either datum - refused naming which, in the style
 1005:       of the longitude-convention refusal;
 1006:     * a HORIZONTAL job SUPPLIED with either datum - refused too: a datum
 1007:       handed to a job that will never apply or record it is a contradiction,
 1008:       and silently ignoring it would let a caller believe an elevation
 1009:       conversion happened (the mirror of longitude_convention's None-is-a-
 1010:       statement rule);

2026-08-11T00:46:33.630619Z ERROR codex_models_manager::manager: failed to renew cache TTL: missing field `supports_reasoning_summaries` at line 93 column 5
exec
"C:\\Windows\\System32\\WindowsPowerShell\\v1.0\\powershell.exe" -Command '$n=0; Get-Content michspc/job.py | ForEach-Object { $n++; if ($n -ge 1200 -and $n -le 1810) { '"'{0,5}: {1}' -f "'$n, $_ } }' in C:\claude-projects\coord-convert
 succeeded in 481ms:
 1200:             else "Correct the file, or the convention selected for it."
 1201:         )
 1202:         raise ValueError(
 1203:             f"{context}: the longitude column reads {longitude_as_written!r}, "
 1204:             f"which under the '{convention}' convention this job states is a "
 1205:             f"signed longitude of {longitude!r} - outside the -180 to 180 "
 1206:             f"range a signed longitude can have. A value between 180 and 360 "
 1207:             f"is the 0-360 EAST convention, which this program deliberately "
 1208:             f"does not read from a file: 275.5 east and -84.5 both name the "
 1209:             f"same meridian, and the NGS grid files use 0-360 internally, so "
 1210:             f"an unconverted 0-360 longitude would look up plausible geoid "
 1211:             f"and VERTCON values while the State Plane conversion placed the "
 1212:             f"point thousands of kilometres away. " + advice
 1213:         )
 1214: 
 1215: 
 1216: def per_side_geoid_models(
 1217:     settings: JobSettings, transformation: VerticalTransformation | None
 1218: ) -> tuple[geoid.GeoidModel | None, geoid.GeoidModel | None]:
 1219:     """``(input-side model, output-side model)`` for this job, normalized.
 1220: 
 1221:     THE one statement of the per-side pairing (the owner's feature,
 1222:     2026-08-09), read by the computation, the factors rule, the swap
 1223:     detection and the record, so no two of them can pair differently.
 1224: 
 1225:     The normalization SUPERSEDES DESIGN.md #41's either-endpoint contortion
 1226:     by generalization: each side's model must match its own side's datum.
 1227:     The one pre-existing call shape that put a source-era model in
 1228:     ``geoid_model`` - a modeled transformation whose SOURCE datum is the
 1229:     model's era but whose target is not (NAVD88 -> NGVD29 with GEOID18, the
 1230:     configuration #41 widened the rule for) - is treated as the input-side
 1231:     model here, so every #41-era caller keeps working, its factors and
 1232:     outputs bit-identical, and ``factors_use_source_era`` becomes a
 1233:     consequence of the pairing rather than a parallel rule.
 1234:     """
 1235:     source_model = settings.source_geoid_model
 1236:     output_model = settings.geoid_model
 1237:     if (
 1238:         transformation is not None
 1239:         and not transformation.is_identity
 1240:         and output_model is not None
 1241:         and source_model is None
 1242:         and output_model.vertical_datum.code == transformation.source.code
 1243:         and output_model.vertical_datum.code != transformation.target.code
 1244:     ):
 1245:         return output_model, None
 1246:     return source_model, output_model
 1247: 
 1248: 
 1249: def factors_geoid_model(
 1250:     settings: JobSettings, transformation: VerticalTransformation | None
 1251: ) -> geoid.GeoidModel | None:
 1252:     """The model this job's factors are computed from, or None.
 1253: 
 1254:     The side whose height and separation share an era: the OUTPUT side where
 1255:     it has a model - the factors then describe the height the Z column
 1256:     carries - else the INPUT side, else no model at all (the factors read
 1257:     N/A). One statement, shared by ``run``, the record and the audit CSV's
 1258:     "Geoid model" column, so they cannot name different grids.
 1259:     """
 1260:     source_model, output_model = per_side_geoid_models(settings, transformation)
 1261:     return output_model if output_model is not None else source_model
 1262: 
 1263: 
 1264: def geoid_swap_models(
 1265:     settings: JobSettings, transformation: VerticalTransformation | None
 1266: ) -> tuple[geoid.GeoidModel, geoid.GeoidModel] | None:
 1267:     """``(input model, output model)`` when this job converts BETWEEN geoid
 1268:     models, else None.
 1269: 
 1270:     A geoid-to-geoid conversion is exactly: an identity vertical
 1271:     transformation (the datum does not change) with a model stated on each
 1272:     side and the two models different. The same model on both sides is the
 1273:     ordinary identity job - no swap step, no record - and a modeled
 1274:     transformation with both sides stated was refused before any point
 1275:     converted (``run``'s compound-job refusal).
 1276:     """
 1277:     if transformation is None or not transformation.is_identity:
 1278:         return None
 1279:     source_model, output_model = per_side_geoid_models(settings, transformation)
 1280:     if source_model is None or output_model is None:
 1281:         return None
 1282:     if source_model == output_model:
 1283:         return None
 1284:     return source_model, output_model
 1285: 
 1286: 
 1287: def factors_use_source_era(
 1288:     settings: JobSettings, transformation: VerticalTransformation | None
 1289: ) -> bool:
 1290:     """Whether this job's factors are computed from the SOURCE-datum height.
 1291: 
 1292:     The #41 either-endpoint rule's one configuration where the factors do not
 1293:     use the height the Z column carries - now a CONSEQUENCE of the per-side
 1294:     pairing (``per_side_geoid_models``): the factors follow the one side
 1295:     that has a model, and on a modeled transformation only the input side
 1296:     can honestly hold one whose era is the source datum's. Stated once and
 1297:     called from both the computation (``_convert_row``) and the record
 1298:     (``report.py``'s Factor height paragraph), so the sentence and the
 1299:     arithmetic cannot drift apart (WP-V7 review gate, LOW 2; one
 1300:     authoritative representation per fact).
 1301:     """
 1302:     if transformation is None or transformation.is_identity:
 1303:         return False
 1304:     source_model, output_model = per_side_geoid_models(settings, transformation)
 1305:     return output_model is None and source_model is not None
 1306: 
 1307: 
 1308: def _convert_row(
 1309:     row: pnezd.PnezdRow,
 1310:     settings: JobSettings,
 1311:     grid,
 1312:     transformation: VerticalTransformation | None,
 1313:     vertcon_grids: vertcon.VertconGridPair | None,
 1314:     swap_grids: _GeoidSwapGrids | None = None,
 1315: ) -> ConvertedPoint:
 1316:     context = f"point {row.point_id}"
 1317:     warnings: list[ConversionWarning] = []
 1318: 
 1319:     geodetic_input = settings.direction is Direction.GEODETIC_TO_ZONE or (
 1320:         settings.direction is Direction.VERTICAL_ONLY
 1321:         and settings.source_zone is None
 1322:     )
 1323: 
 1324:     if geodetic_input:
 1325:         # The file's "northing" and "easting" columns hold latitude and
 1326:         # longitude. The longitude is normalised to the program's signed
 1327:         # convention here, at the boundary, and nowhere else.
 1328:         latitude = row.northing
 1329:         longitude = settings.longitude_convention.to_signed(row.easting)
 1330:         _require_geodetic_in_range(
 1331:             latitude, longitude, row.easting, settings, context
 1332:         )
 1333:         if settings.direction is Direction.GEODETIC_TO_ZONE:
 1334:             conversion = project_point(
 1335:                 latitude,
 1336:                 longitude,
 1337:                 settings.geodetic_frame,
 1338:                 settings.target_zone,
 1339:                 context,
 1340:             )
 1341:             output_unit = settings.output_unit
 1342:             output_northing = output_unit.from_meters(conversion.target_northing)
 1343:             output_easting = output_unit.from_meters(conversion.target_easting)
 1344:         else:
 1345:             # VERTICAL_ONLY, geodetic input. No zone exists anywhere in this
 1346:             # job, and none is fabricated: the position record carries the
 1347:             # pivot for the grid lookups and None for every zone-derived
 1348:             # quantity, so the grid scale and combined factors downstream are
 1349:             # honestly absent rather than invented. The output coordinate
 1350:             # columns are the INPUT values, unchanged - the mirror this
 1351:             # direction promises - including the longitude exactly as the
 1352:             # file wrote it, in its own convention.
 1353:             conversion = geodetic_position(
 1354:                 latitude, longitude, settings.geodetic_frame, context
 1355:             )
 1356:             output_northing = row.northing
 1357:             output_easting = row.easting
 1358:     else:
 1359:         northing_m = settings.input_unit.to_meters(row.northing)
 1360:         easting_m = settings.input_unit.to_meters(row.easting)
 1361: 
 1362:         if easting_looks_wrong_for_zone(easting_m, settings.source_zone):
 1363:             warnings.append(
 1364:                 ConversionWarning(
 1365:                     code=WarningCode.EASTING_UNLIKE_SELECTED_ZONE,
 1366:                     message=(
 1367:                         f"{context}: an easting of {row.easting:,.3f} "
 1368:                         f"{settings.input_unit.code} does not look like "
 1369:                         f"{settings.source_zone.name} data, whose eastings sit "
 1370:                         f"near {settings.input_unit.from_meters(settings.source_zone.definition.easting_origin):,.0f} "

 1451:     # The ordering's effect on the elevation factor is ~0.02 ppm - negligible,
 1452:     # and nobody should mistake the factor for the reason. The reason is the
 1453:     # Z VALUE ITSELF: an unshifted height is out by ~0.46 ft across Michigan

 1466:         # catches NO record arriving on settings that promise a shift. Without

 1471:         # hold, not run()'s good manners.
 1472:         raise ValueError(

 1474:             f"({settings.source_vertical_datum.code} -> "

 1501:                     # ellipsoid height held fixed, the orthometric height
 1502:                     # re-derived under the output model -
 1503:                     # H_out = H_in + N_in - N_out. Runs only on an identity

 1528:                         vertical_reading = None

 1530:                             ConversionWarning(

 """What columns 3 and 4 of the audit CSV are called, per direction.
 
 The linear pair is the header for every direction whose input file holds grid
 coordinates, and it is deliberately unchanged. The geodetic pair replaces it
 only when the input file's columns two and three hold degrees, because

 """

+def vertical_shift_and_sigma_m(point) -> tuple[float | None, float | None]:
+    """``(shift, sigma)`` in metres for a point's shift and sigma cells, or
+    None where no number exists.
+
+    THE one statement of which numbers those two columns carry, shared by
+    the audit CSV (``audit_rows``) and the on-screen Multi point table
+    (``results_model.row_strings``), so the two surfaces cannot disagree:
+
+    * a geoid-to-geoid point carries the SWAP shift - the value that moved
+      the height, ``GeoidSwapReading.shift_m`` - and no sigma: NGS
+      publishes no error model for the difference between two of its geoid
+      models, so N/A is the only honest cell (docs/DESIGN.md #36's rule,
+      arriving at a new absence);
+    * every other point with a ``VerticalReading`` carries the datum shift
+      and its sigma, exactly as before;
+    * a point with neither reading - no elevation, or a coverage refusal -
+      carries neither number.
+    """
+    if point.geoid_swap is not None:
+        return point.geoid_swap.shift_m, None
+    if point.vertical is not None:
+        return point.vertical.shift_m, point.vertical.sigma_m
+    return None, None
+
+
 def vertical_shift_heading(unit) -> str:
     """``Vertical shift (ift)`` - the audit CSV's shift column heading.
 
     THE authoritative wording: the Multi point table and the Single point
     panel's shift-row suffix carry this unit code too, per #17's standing
     choice of one wording on every surface (the table imports this function
     rather than restating it). The unit passed is the JOB'S INPUT UNIT - the
     owner's instruction (2026-08-09): the shift is read against the elevation
     the surveyor supplied, so it is stated in the unit that elevation was
     typed in. The value under this heading converts with it, in
     ``audit_rows`` - a heading claiming feet over a metre value would be the
     worst outcome of this change, so the two are built from the same unit
     object and pinned together.
     """
     return f"Vertical shift ({unit.code})"
 
 
 def vertical_sigma_heading(unit) -> str:
     """``Shift sigma (ift)`` - the sigma column heading, same rule as the
     shift's: the job's input unit, one wording on every surface (#17), the
     cell converted by the same unit object."""
     return f"Shift sigma ({unit.code})"

 def _expected_coordinates(result: JobResult, point) -> tuple[str, float, str, float]:
     """What the export's columns two and three must carry for this point.
 
     Returns ``(name, value, name, value)`` - the label the round-trip refusal

     different columns.

+def _geoid_swap_method_block(swap_models) -> list[str]:
+    """The METHOD section's account of a geoid-to-geoid conversion.
+
+    Facts only, stated plainly (the owner's instruction, 2026-08-09, the
+    #33/#34/#45 ruling extended to the written record): the two models with
+    their tile filenames and digests resolved from the registry - the same
+    records the loader authenticates against - and the arithmetic. No
+    caveat prose and no uncertainty lecture; the sigma surfaces simply read
+    N/A.
+    """
+    source_record = geoid_model_by_name(swap_models[0].name)
+    target_record = geoid_model_by_name(swap_models[1].name)
+    lines: list[str] = []
+    add = lines.append
+
+    add("GEOID CHANGE")
+    add("")
+    add(
+        f"Input geoid        {source_record.name}, NGS grid tile "
+        f"{source_record.tile_filename}"
+    )
+    add(f"                   SHA-256 {source_record.sha256}")
+    add(
+        f"Output geoid       {target_record.name}, NGS grid tile "
+        f"{target_record.tile_filename}"
+    )
+    add(f"                   SHA-256 {target_record.sha256}")
+    lines.extend(
+        _labelled_paragraph(
+            "Arithmetic",
+            f"The ellipsoid height is held fixed and the orthometric height "
+            f"re-derived under the output model: H_out = H_in + N_in - "
+            f"N_out, where N_in and N_out are the {source_record.name} and "
+            f"{target_record.name} geoid heights at the point's horizontal "
+            f"position, in metres. The Z column carries H_out; the _full.csv "
+            f"export's Vertical shift column carries N_in - N_out per "
+            f"point.",
+        )
+    )
+    add("")
+    return lines
+
+
+def _geoid_swap_elevation_block(result, transformation, swap_models, unit) -> list[str]:
+    """The ELEVATIONS section's account of a geoid-to-geoid conversion.
+
+    The ``_factor_summary`` shape over the per-point geoid change, in the
+    job's INPUT unit (the 2026-08-09 units rule; on this direction-less
+    identity the input and output units can still differ on a
+    Horizontal + Vertical job, and the shift columns follow the input one).
+    The sigma line is the bare ``N/A`` of the summary shape - no number
+    exists and nothing is attached to the absence (the owner's instruction,
+    2026-08-09).
+    """
+    source_name = swap_models[0].name
+    target_name = swap_models[1].name
+    points = result.points
+    swapped = [p for p in points if p.geoid_swap is not None]
+    lines: list[str] = []
+    add = lines.append
+
+    if not swapped:
+        lines.extend(
+            textwrap.wrap(
+                f"0 of {len(points)} points had their elevation re-derived "
+                f"from {source_name} to {target_name}: no point carried a "
+                f"convertible elevation. The causes are itemized above; any "
+                f"that raise warnings are repeated under WARNINGS. The "
+                f"_full.csv export's shift and sigma cells read "
+                f"{fmt.NOT_AVAILABLE} for every point.",
+                width=78,
+            )
+        )
+        return lines
+
+    lines.extend(
+        textwrap.wrap(
+            f"{len(swapped)} of {len(points)} points had their elevation "
+            f"re-derived from the {source_name} geoid model to the "
+            f"{target_name} geoid model; both vertical datums are "
+            f"{transformation.source.code}, so no datum shift was applied "
+            f"(see METHOD). Each point's geoid change is in the _full.csv "
+            f"export's Vertical shift column. Summary across this job:",
+            width=78,
+        )
+    )
+    add("")
+
+    shifts = [p.geoid_swap.shift_m for p in swapped]

 def _vertical_elevation_block(result, transformation) -> list[str]:
     """The ELEVATIONS section's account of what the vertical conversion did.
 
     The record keeps a SUMMARY of the per-point sigma - min, max, mean, the
     shape ``_factor_summary`` uses for the scale factors - and leaves the
     per-point column to the audit CSV: the record says how uncertain this job
     was, the CSV says how uncertain each point was (plan section 5.1).

                 f"any of them (see METHOD). No model ran, so no modeled "
                 f"uncertainty is introduced.",

             )

         return lines

 def _point_id_block(points, per_line: int = 8) -> list[str]:
     """Point identifiers wrapped to a readable width."""
     ids = [p.point_id for p in points]
     lines = []
     for start in range(0, len(ids), per_line):
         lines.append("    " + ", ".join(ids[start : start + per_line]))
     return lines

  637:     # this gates is additive and swap-only: a job without a swap keeps every
  638:     # byte it wrote before the feature.
  639:     swap_models = geoid_swap_models(settings, transformation)
  640: 
  641:     lines: list[str] = []
  642:     add = lines.append
  643:

  661:         # No digest means no bytes passed through this program: the rows were

  663:         # available, and it is a great deal more honest than the hash of
  664:         # whatever happens to sit at the path above - which is what this line

  200: INPUT_GEOID_LABEL = "Input geoid:"

  202: """The geoid selectors' labels (the owner's per-side feature, 2026-08-09).
  203: 
  204: In the two vertical modes the input and output geoid models are chosen
  205: separately: the tab's existing combo becomes the OUTPUT side under
  206: ``OUTPUT_GEOID_LABEL``, and a second combo appears under
  207: ``INPUT_GEOID_LABEL``. In Horizontal mode the single combo keeps

  212: 
  213: def vertical_mode_buttons(
  214:     parent, on_change
  215: ) -> tuple[QRadioButton, QRadioButton, QRadioButton, QButtonGroup]:
  216:     """The Horizontal / Horizontal + Vertical / Vertical toggle, one per tab.
  217: 
  218:     Three ``QRadioButton``s in an exclusive ``QButtonGroup`` - the repo's
  219:     existing idiom (``elevation_in_file``), native per METHOD.md section 5.
  220:     Built here so both tabs get the *same* control rather than a lookalike
  221:     set, exactly as ``longitude_combo`` serves them both; the handler is
  222:     passed in because the tabs share no state (docs/DESIGN.md amendment #26)
  223:     and each connects its own.
  224: 
  225:     Opens on Horizontal (plan section 4.1): today's behaviour, asserting
  226:     nothing about a vertical datum.
  227: 
  228:     The handler rides ``QButtonGroup.buttonToggled``, filtered to the button
  229:     being CHECKED. With two buttons the old wiring - one button's ``toggled``
  230:     - fired exactly once per mode change, because every change toggled both.
  231:     Three exclusive buttons break that arithmetic: a change toggles only the
  232:     button leaving and the button entering, so any single button's signal
  233:     misses the switches it is not part of (Horizontal's ``toggled`` never
  234:     fires on a Horizontal + Vertical <-> Vertical switch - a mode change
  235:     that would silently keep a stale result on screen, the amendment #26
  236:     CRITICAL class). The group's signal sees every change; the checked
  237:     filter keeps the count at exactly one call per change.
  238:     """
  239:     horizontal = QRadioButton(HORIZONTAL_MODE_TEXT, parent)
  240:     vertical = QRadioButton(VERTICAL_MODE_TEXT, parent)
  241:     vertical_only = QRadioButton(VERTICAL_ONLY_MODE_TEXT, parent)
  242:     group = QButtonGroup(parent)
  243:     group.addButton(horizontal)
  244:     group.addButton(vertical)
  245:     group.addButton(vertical_only)
  246:     # Checked BEFORE the connection, so building the control does not fire
  247:     # the handler on a tab that is still constructing itself.
  248:     horizontal.setChecked(True)
  249: 
  250:     def _on_button_toggled(_button, checked: bool) -> None:
  251:         if checked:
  252:             on_change()
  253: 
  254:     group.buttonToggled.connect(_on_button_toggled)
  255:     return horizontal, vertical, vertical_only, group
  256: 
  257: 
  258: def vertical_mode_for(
  259:     horizontal: QRadioButton,
  260:     vertical: QRadioButton,
  261:     vertical_only: QRadioButton,
  262: ) -> VerticalMode:
  263:     """The mode a tab's toggle currently states.
  264: 
  265:     The rule lives here, beside the builder, so the two tabs cannot read the
  266:     same set of buttons two different ways. Exactly one button is checked at
  267:     all times - the group is exclusive and the builder checks Horizontal - so
  268:     the none-checked branch below is unreachable through the interface; it
  269:     refuses rather than guessing because a mode guessed here would silently
  270:     decide whether elevations, coordinates, or both are converted.
  271:     """
  272:     if horizontal.isChecked():
  273:         return VerticalMode.HORIZONTAL
  274:     if vertical.isChecked():
  275:         return VerticalMode.HORIZONTAL_AND_VERTICAL
  276:     if vertical_only.isChecked():
  277:         return VerticalMode.VERTICAL
  278:     raise ValueError(
  279:         "No mode button is checked, so the vertical mode is unknown. "
  280:         "The toggle is built with Horizontal checked and the group is "
  281:         "exclusive, so this state should be unreachable; refusing rather "
  282:         "than assuming a mode."
  283:     )
  284: 
  285: 
  286: def vertical_datum_combo(parent, on_change) -> QComboBox:
  287:     """A vertical datum dropdown, built from the registry.
  288: 
  289:     Opens **unanswered**, per docs/DESIGN.md section 7: NGVD 29 and NAVD 88
  290:     heights differ by up to 0.41 m across Michigan while looking identical,
  291:     so the two entries are indistinguishable from the numbers on screen and a
  292:     preselected one would be an answer to a question the user was never
  293:     asked. Amendment #29's positive-west preselect is a narrow, recorded
  294:     exception; this control is not it (plan section 4.2).
  295: 
  296:     Only USABLE datums are offered, by asking each record rather than naming
  297:     names: NAPGD2022 is declared-not-usable in ``spc.vertical`` and must not
  298:     appear until its status changes there - at which point it appears with no
  299:     interface change, the property ``zone_combo`` already has. Both usable
  300:     datums are offered on both ends: an identity pair (NAVD88 to NAVD88) is a
  301:     legitimate job that *states* the datum, and the registry, not this
  302:     dropdown, owns which pairs convert.
  303:     """
  304:     combo = QComboBox(parent)
  305:     combo.addItem("â€” choose â€”", UNCHOSEN)
  306:     for datum in ALL_VERTICAL_DATUMS:
  307:         if datum.is_usable:
  308:             # "National Geodetic Vertical Datum of 1929 (NGVD29)" - the
  309:             # record's own name and code, via its __str__, never typed here.
  310:             combo.addItem(str(datum), datum)
  311:     combo.currentIndexChanged.connect(on_change)
  312:     return combo
  313: 
  314: 
  315: def vertical_datum_for(data) -> VerticalDatum | None:
  316:     """The datum a dropdown's current data names, or None while unanswered."""
  317:     return data if isinstance(data, VerticalDatum) else None
  318: 
  319: 
  320: def geoid_combo(parent) -> QComboBox:
  321:     """A geoid model dropdown, built from the registry.
  322: 
  323:     Every model in ``geoid.ALL_GEOID_MODELS``, in declaration order, and
  324:     nothing else - a model added to the registry appears with no interface
  325:     change (plan section 4.3). **No "none" entry**, the owner's decision
  326:     (plan section 5): the core can state ``geoid_model=None``, but no
  327:     interface offers it.
  328: 
  329:     Opens on GEOID18, the model this program has shipped since 0.1.0. A
  330:     default is defensible here as it is for the units and not for the
  331:     vertical datums: the model in force is named in the job record and the
  332:     audit CSV, so the answer is stated in every output rather than silently
  333:     assumed. No handler parameter, matching ``unit_combo``: the Single point
  334:     tab connects its own invalidation, and the Multi point tab - whose table
  335:     describes a written archive, not the current controls - connects nothing.
  336:     """
  337:     combo = QComboBox(parent)
  338:     for model in geoid.ALL_GEOID_MODELS:
  339:         combo.addItem(model.name, model)
  340:     combo.setCurrentIndex(combo.findData(geoid.GEOID18_MODEL))
  341:     combo.setToolTip(
  342:         "Geoid separation is looked up per point from the selected model's "
  343:         "bundled grid."
  344:     )
  345:     return combo
  346: 
  347: 
  348: def geoid_models_for_datum(datum) -> tuple:
  349:     """Every registry model publishing separations for this datum's heights.
  350: 
  351:     The registry's own ``vertical_datum`` field is what filters - the same
  352:     fact ``job.run``'s per-side era guard refuses on - so a side's dropdown
  353:     can never offer a model the job would refuse for that side. ``None``
  354:     (an unanswered datum dropdown) filters to nothing: a side whose datum
  355:     is unknown cannot say which models apply. A future datum's models
  356:     appear here the day their registry records do, with no interface
  357:     change - the ``zone_combo`` property.
  358:     """
  359:     if datum is None:
  360:         return ()
  361:     return tuple(
  362:         model
  363:         for model in geoid.ALL_GEOID_MODELS
  364:         if model.vertical_datum.code == datum.code
  365:     )
  366: 
  367: 
  368: def refresh_geoid_combo(combo, models) -> None:
  369:     """Make a side's geoid combo offer exactly ``models``.
  370: 
  371:     **Disabled - grayed - with its items cleared when ``models`` is empty**,
  372:     the owner's explicit word (2026-08-09): a side whose datum has no
  373:     published geoid model (NGVD 29 today) is a question that APPLIES and is
  374:     unanswerable, so the control stays visible and gray rather than hidden -
  375:     the hidden idiom is for controls that do not apply at all. Enabled-but-
  376:     empty would be worse than either: an enabled control promises a choice
  377:     it cannot offer.
  378: 
  379:     A side with models enables and opens on GEOID18 where the filter keeps
  380:     it, preserving the user's own selection when it survives a refresh so
  381:     flipping a mode or an unrelated datum does not silently discard an
  382:     answer. No-ops when the offering and enablement are already right, so
  383:     the many paths that refresh cost nothing - and fire no signals - when
  384:     nothing changed.
  385:     """
  386:     models = tuple(models)
  387:     offered = tuple(combo.itemData(i) for i in range(combo.count()))
  388:     if offered == models and combo.isEnabled() == bool(models):
  389:         return
  390: 
  391:     previous = combo.currentData()
  392:     combo.clear()
  393:     for model in models:
  394:         combo.addItem(model.name, model)
  395:     if not models:
  396:         combo.setEnabled(False)
  397:         return
  398:     combo.setEnabled(True)
  399:     preferred = (
  400:         previous
  401:         if previous in models
  402:         else geoid.GEOID18_MODEL
  403:         if geoid.GEOID18_MODEL in models
  404:         else models[0]
  405:     )
  406:     combo.setCurrentIndex(combo.findData(preferred))
  407: 
  408: 
  409: def direction_for(source_data, target_data) -> Direction | None:
  410:     """The job this pair of dropdowns describes, or None if it is not one.
  411: 
  412:     Returns None while either side is unanswered, and for the one
  413:     combination that is not a conversion: geodetic to geodetic, which has
  414:     no zone at either end and nothing to project through.
  415: 
WINDOW
  320:         # zone" dropdown would be a question the job never asks.

  401:         self.geoid_label = QLabel(GEOID_MODEL_LABEL, box)
  402:         self.geoid_combo = geoid_combo(box)

  417:         self.output_edit = QLineEdit(box)
  418:         # Empty, with no placeholder and no default: the owner reversed
  419:         # amendment #16 note 3 (docs/DESIGN.md amendment #27). Downloads is not
  420:         # where a survey job's exports belong, and a pre-filled destination is
  421:         # answered by pressing Convert rather than by choosing. Convert stays
  422:         # disabled until this is filled, so the empty field is a question the
  423:         # program refuses to answer for him rather than an obstacle.
  424:         self.output_edit.textChanged.connect(self._update_convert_enabled)
  425:         self.output_browse = QPushButton("...", box)

  490: 
  491:     @property
  492:     def output_directory(self) -> Path | None:
  493:         text = self.output_edit.text().strip()
  494:         return Path(text) if text else None
  495: 
  496:     def direction(self) -> Direction | None:
  497:         """This tab's job, per ``controls.direction_for``, which owns the rule."""
  498:         return direction_for(self.from_zone.currentData(), self.to_zone.currentData())
  499: 
  500:     def longitude_convention(self) -> LongitudeConvention | None:
  501:         data = self.longitude_combo.currentData()
  502:         return data if isinstance(data, LongitudeConvention) else None
  503: 
  504:     def vertical_mode(self) -> VerticalMode:
  505:         """What the mode toggle states; ``controls.vertical_mode_for`` owns the rule."""
  506:         return vertical_mode_for(
  507:             self.mode_horizontal, self.mode_vertical, self.mode_vertical_only
  508:         )
  509: 
  510:     def source_vertical_datum(self) -> VerticalDatum | None:
  511:         return vertical_datum_for(self.vertical_source_combo.currentData())
  512: 
  513:     def target_vertical_datum(self) -> VerticalDatum | None:
  514:         return vertical_datum_for(self.vertical_target_combo.currentData())
  515: 
  516:     def output_geoid_model(self) -> geoid.GeoidModel | None:
  517:         """The model ``JobSettings.geoid_model`` gets from this tab.
  518: 
  519:         Horizontal mode reads the combo exactly as it always has - the full
  520:         registry list always holds a model. In the vertical modes the combo
  521:         is the OUTPUT side, filtered per datum, and a grayed side states
  522:         None: its datum has no published geoid model, so no model is
  523:         emitted rather than one the job would refuse (the per-side rule the
  524:         graying exists to state).
  525:         """
  526:         if not self.vertical_mode().converts_elevations:
  527:             return self.geoid_combo.currentData()
  528:         data = (
  529:             self.geoid_combo.currentData()
  530:             if self.geoid_combo.isEnabled()
  531:             else None
  532:         )
  533:         return data if isinstance(data, geoid.GeoidModel) else None
  534: 
  535:     def input_geoid_model(self) -> geoid.GeoidModel | None:
  536:         """The model ``JobSettings.source_geoid_model`` gets from this tab.
  537: 
  538:         None in Horizontal mode - the hidden combo is never read, the
  539:         datum-row idiom - and None from a grayed side in the vertical
  540:         modes, for the reason ``output_geoid_model`` states.
  541:         """
  542:         if not self.vertical_mode().converts_elevations:
  543:             return None
  544:         data = (
  545:             self.input_geoid_combo.currentData()
  546:             if self.input_geoid_combo.isEnabled()
  547:             else None
  548:         )
  549:         return data if isinstance(data, geoid.GeoidModel) else None
  550: 
  551:     def settings(self) -> JobSettings | None:
  552:         """Assemble the job settings, or None if the form is not yet complete."""
  553:         if self.input_path is None or self.output_directory is None:
  554:             return None
  555: 
  556:         if self.vertical_mode() is VerticalMode.VERTICAL:
  557:             return self._vertical_only_settings()
  558: 
  559:         direction = self.direction()
  560:         if direction is None:
  561:             return None
  562: 
  563:         # A vertical job needs both datums answered before it is a job at all
  564:         # (plan section 4.4); a horizontal job states None for both, which is
  565:         # the statement job.run requires of it - a horizontal job supplied
  566:         # with a datum is refused there. The refusal matrix itself stays in
  567:         # job.run: this method only decides whether the form is complete,
  568:         # never whether a completed form's combination is convertible, so a
  569:         # pair the registry refuses reaches the job and comes back as the
  570:         # registry's own teaching message rather than a greyed-out control.
  571:         mode = self.vertical_mode()
  572:         if mode is VerticalMode.HORIZONTAL_AND_VERTICAL:
  573:             source_datum = self.source_vertical_datum()
  574:             target_datum = self.target_vertical_datum()
  575:             if source_datum is None or target_datum is None:
  576:                 return None
  577:         else:
  578:             source_datum = None
  579:             target_datum = None
  580: 
  581:         source = self.from_zone.currentData()
  582:         target = self.to_zone.currentData()
  583:         source_zone = source if isinstance(source, Zone) else None
  584:         target_zone = target if isinstance(target, Zone) else None
  585: 
  586:         common = dict(
  587:             input_path=self.input_path,
  588:             output_directory=self.output_directory,
  589:             direction=direction,
  590:             source_zone=source_zone,
  591:             target_zone=target_zone,
  592:             input_unit=self.input_unit.currentData(),
  593:             output_unit=self.output_unit.currentData(),
  594:             # From the dropdowns (WP-V8, plan section 4.3; per-side since
  595:             # the owner's 2026-08-09 feature), exactly as the two unit
  596:             # combos are read: the combos offer only registry records, and
  597:             # job.run refuses an impostor by name. A grayed side emits
  598:             # None - its datum has no published model.
  599:             geoid_model=self.output_geoid_model(),
  600:             source_geoid_model=self.input_geoid_model(),
  601:             vertical_mode=mode,
  602:             source_vertical_datum=source_datum,
  603:             target_vertical_datum=target_datum,
  604:         )
  605: 
  606:         if direction is Direction.ZONE_TO_ZONE:
  607:             # A pure zone-to-zone job never consults the longitude convention
  608:             # (michspc.job._convert_row), so the interface does not pretend the
  609:             # user answered a question it never asked. The field carries no
  610:             # default, so the absence has to be stated rather than omitted.
  611:             return JobSettings(**common, longitude_convention=None)
  612: 
  613:         convention = self.longitude_convention()
  614:         if convention is None:
  615:             return None
  616:         return JobSettings(**common, longitude_convention=convention)
  617: 
  618:     def _vertical_only_settings(self) -> JobSettings | None:
  619:         """The vertical-only job this tab's controls describe, or None.
  620: 
  621:         The From selection is the INPUT system - a zone, or the Geodetic
  622:         entry stated as ``source_zone=None`` - and there is no output system:
  623:         ``target_zone`` is None and ``output_unit`` IS the input unit, because
  624:         the exports reproduce the input's columns and ``job.run`` refuses a
  625:         mismatch. The To dropdown and the output unit selector are hidden in
  626:         this mode and deliberately not read. The longitude convention follows
  627:         the same rule the core enforces: stated for a geodetic input, None
  628:         for a zone input.
  629:         """
  630:         source = self.from_zone.currentData()
  631:         if source == UNCHOSEN:
  632:             return None
  633:         source_zone = source if isinstance(source, Zone) else None
  634: 
  635:         source_datum = self.source_vertical_datum()
  636:         target_datum = self.target_vertical_datum()
  637:         if source_datum is None or target_datum is None:
  638:             return None
  639: 
  640:         input_unit = self.input_unit.currentData()
  641:         common = dict(
  642:             input_path=self.input_path,
  643:             output_directory=self.output_directory,
  644:             direction=Direction.VERTICAL_ONLY,
  645:             source_zone=source_zone,
  646:             target_zone=None,
  647:             input_unit=input_unit,
  648:             output_unit=input_unit,
  649:             geoid_model=self.output_geoid_model(),
  650:             source_geoid_model=self.input_geoid_model(),
  651:             vertical_mode=VerticalMode.VERTICAL,
  652:             source_vertical_datum=source_datum,
  653:             target_vertical_datum=target_datum,
  654:         )
  655: 
  656:         if source_zone is not None:
  657:             # The file carries no longitudes and none are written - the
  658:             # zone-to-zone statement rule, which job.run enforces.
  659:             return JobSettings(**common, longitude_convention=None)
  660: 
  661:         convention = self.longitude_convention()
  662:         if convention is None:
  663:             return None
  664:         return JobSettings(**common, longitude_convention=convention)
  665: 
  666:     # ------------------------------------------------------------------
  667:     # Enablement
  668:     # ------------------------------------------------------------------
  669: 
  670:     def _on_direction_changed(self) -> None:
  671:         self._update_input_hint()
  672:         self._update_unit_labels()
  673:         self._update_longitude_relevance()
  674:         self._update_convert_enabled()
  675: 
  676:     def _update_unit_labels(self) -> None:
  677:         """Say what each unit selector governs, given From and To.
  678: 
  679:         Reads the two dropdowns' own data, not ``direction()``: each selector
  680:         belongs to one END of the job and must describe that end even while the
  681:         other end is still unanswered. Nothing here computes anything - it sets
  682:         two strings and two tooltips (see ``UNITS_LABEL_ELEVATION_ONLY`` for why
  683:         neither selector is ever disabled).
  684:         """
  685:         source_is_geodetic = self.from_zone.currentData() == GEODETIC
  686:         target_is_geodetic = self.to_zone.currentData() == GEODETIC
  687: 
  688:         self.input_unit_label.setText(
  689:             UNITS_LABEL_ELEVATION_ONLY if source_is_geodetic else UNITS_LABEL
  690:         )
  691:         self.input_unit.setToolTip(
  692:             UNITS_TOOLTIP_GEODETIC_IN if source_is_geodetic else UNITS_TOOLTIP_ZONE_IN
  693:         )
  694:         self.output_unit_label.setText(
  695:             UNITS_LABEL_ELEVATION_ONLY if target_is_geodetic else UNITS_LABEL
  696:         )
  697:         self.output_unit.setToolTip(
  698:             UNITS_TOOLTIP_GEODETIC_OUT if target_is_geodetic else UNITS_TOOLTIP_ZONE_OUT
  699:         )
  700: 
  701:     def input_hint_text(self) -> str:
  702:         """The column layout the input file will be read as, given From.
  703: 
  704:         Reads the From selection only. The To selection cannot change how the
  705:         input is parsed, and a hint that moved with it would be describing the
  706:         wrong end of the job.
  707:         """
  708:         source = self.from_zone.currentData()
  709:         if source == GEODETIC:
  710:             return INPUT_HINT_GEODETIC
  711:         if isinstance(source, Zone):
  712:             return INPUT_HINT_ZONE
  713:         return INPUT_HINT_UNCHOSEN
  714: 
  715:     def _update_input_hint(self) -> None:
  716:         self.input_hint.setText(self.input_hint_text())
  717: 
  718:     def _update_longitude_relevance(self) -> None:
  719:         """The selector matters only when geodetic coordinates are involved;
  720:         ``controls.longitude_relevance`` owns the rule for all three modes."""
  721:         relevant = longitude_relevance(
  722:             self.vertical_mode(), self.from_zone.currentData(), self.direction()
  723:         )
  724:         self.longitude_label.setEnabled(relevant)
  725:         self.longitude_combo.setEnabled(relevant)
  726: 
  727:     def _on_vertical_mode_changed(self) -> None:
  728:         self._update_vertical_rows()
  729:         self._update_longitude_relevance()
  730:         self._update_convert_enabled()
  731: 
  732:     def _on_vertical_datum_changed(self) -> None:
  733:         """What both datum combos are wired to: a datum decides which geoid
  734:         models its side may offer (the per-side registry filter), so the
  735:         geoid combos refresh with it - then the gate, as before."""
  736:         self._refresh_geoid_sides()
  737:         self._update_convert_enabled()
  738: 
  739:     def _refresh_geoid_sides(self) -> None:
  740:         """Make both geoid combos offer what the mode and datums allow.
  741: 
  742:         The one place the per-side filter meets this tab's controls
  743:         (``controls.geoid_models_for_datum`` owns the rule): in Horizontal
  744:         the single combo keeps the full registry list under its standing
  745:         label; in the vertical modes each side filters by its own datum,
  746:         and a side with no models is DISABLED - grayed, the owner's word -
  747:         rather than hidden or left enabled over an empty list.
  748:         """
  749:         mode = self.vertical_mode()
  750:         if not mode.converts_elevations:
  751:             self.geoid_label.setText(GEOID_MODEL_LABEL)
  752:             refresh_geoid_combo(self.geoid_combo, geoid.ALL_GEOID_MODELS)
  753:             refresh_geoid_combo(
  754:                 self.input_geoid_combo,
  755:                 geoid_models_for_datum(self.source_vertical_datum()),
  756:             )
  757:             return
  758:         self.geoid_label.setText(OUTPUT_GEOID_LABEL)
  759:         refresh_geoid_combo(
  760:             self.geoid_combo,
  761:             geoid_models_for_datum(self.target_vertical_datum()),
  762:         )
  763:         refresh_geoid_combo(
  764:             self.input_geoid_combo,
  765:             geoid_models_for_datum(self.source_vertical_datum()),
  766:         )
  767: 
  768:     def _update_vertical_rows(self) -> None:
  769:         """Show the datum rows when elevations convert; hide the output
  770:         horizontal controls in vertical-only mode.
  771: 
  772:         Hidden, not disabled, in both directions of this method (plan section
  773:         4.2). The combos keep whatever answer they held, so toggling modes
  774:         does not silently discard a chosen datum or a chosen To zone - but a
  775:         job that does not consult a hidden control never reads it:
  776:         ``settings`` states None for the datums of a horizontal job, and a
  777:         vertical-only job never reads the To zone or the output unit.
  778:         """
  779:         mode = self.vertical_mode()
  780:         for widget in (
  781:             self.vertical_source_label,
  782:             self.vertical_source_combo,
  783:             self.vertical_target_label,
  784:             self.vertical_target_combo,
  785:             # The input-side geoid row is vertical-mode furniture exactly as
  786:             # the datum rows it filters by are: hidden in Horizontal (the
  787:             # question does not apply), visible in both vertical modes -
  788:             # where a side with no models is GRAYED by
  789:             # _refresh_geoid_sides, never hidden (the owner's distinction,
  790:             # 2026-08-09).
  791:             self.input_geoid_label,
  792:             self.input_geoid_combo,
  793:         ):
  794:             widget.setVisible(mode.converts_elevations)
  795:         # The mode also decides each geoid combo's offering and label
  796:         # (Horizontal's full list vs the vertical modes' per-datum filter).
  797:         self._refresh_geoid_sides()
  798:         # The Elevations row is the mirror image (owner's instruction,
  799:         # DESIGN.md #48): in the two vertical modes the elevations must be in
  800:         # the file - the mode exists to convert them - so the "in file"
  801:         # button states the only possibility, and its "passed through
  802:         # unchanged" tooltip would be false there. Hidden with its label and
  803:         # its note; the geoid dropdown beside them stays in every mode.
  804:         for widget in (
  805:             self.elevations_label,
  806:             self.elevation_in_file,
  807:             self.elevation_note,
  808:         ):
  809:             widget.setVisible(not mode.converts_elevations)
  810:         # No output horizontal system exists in vertical-only mode (the To
  811:         # row), and the export mirrors the input's unit (the output Units
  812:         # selector) - visible, either control would be a question this job
  813:         # never asks.
  814:         vertical_only = mode is VerticalMode.VERTICAL
  815:         for widget in (
  816:             self.to_zone_label,
  817:             self.to_zone,
  818:             self.output_unit_label,
  819:             self.output_unit,
  820:         ):
  821:             widget.setVisible(not vertical_only)
  822: 
  823:     def _update_convert_enabled(self) -> None:
  824:         self.convert_button.setEnabled(self.settings() is not None)

  585:     def output_geoid_model(self) -> geoid.GeoidModel | None:
  586:         """The model ``JobSettings.geoid_model`` gets from this tab -
  587:         ``MainWindow.output_geoid_model``'s rule exactly: the full-list
  588:         combo's answer in Horizontal, the filtered OUTPUT side in the
  589:         vertical modes, and None from a grayed side (its datum has no
  590:         published model, so no model is emitted rather than one the job
  591:         would refuse)."""
  592:         if not self.vertical_mode().converts_elevations:
  593:             return self.geoid_combo.currentData()
  594:         data = (
  595:             self.geoid_combo.currentData()
  596:             if self.geoid_combo.isEnabled()
  597:             else None
  598:         )
  599:         return data if isinstance(data, geoid.GeoidModel) else None
  600: 
  601:     def input_geoid_model(self) -> geoid.GeoidModel | None:
  602:         """The model ``JobSettings.source_geoid_model`` gets from this tab:
  603:         None in Horizontal (the hidden combo is never read, the datum-row
  604:         idiom), else the INPUT side's answer, None when grayed."""
  605:         if not self.vertical_mode().converts_elevations:
  606:             return None
  607:         data = (
  608:             self.input_geoid_combo.currentData()
  609:             if self.input_geoid_combo.isEnabled()
  610:             else None
  611:         )
  612:         return data if isinstance(data, geoid.GeoidModel) else None

  614:     def settings(self) -> JobSettings | None:
  615:         """Assemble the job settings, or None if the form is not yet complete.
  616: 
  617:         Mirrors ``MainWindow.settings`` exactly, including the zone-to-zone
  618:         branch that states ``longitude_convention=None`` and the vertical
  619:         branch that gates on both datums, and differs in one respect only:
  620:         ``input_path`` and ``output_directory`` are ``None`` and are not gated
  621:         on. That is a statement, not an absence â€” this job came from no file
  622:         and produces none (docs/DESIGN.md amendment #26).
  623:         """
  624:         if self.vertical_mode() is VerticalMode.VERTICAL:
  625:             return self._vertical_only_settings()
  626: 
  627:         direction = self.direction()
  628:         if direction is None:
  629:             return None
  630: 
  631:         # A vertical job needs both datums answered before it is a job at all
  632:         # (plan section 4.4); a horizontal job states None for both. The
  633:         # refusal matrix stays in job.run: this method decides whether the
  634:         # form is complete, never whether a completed form's combination is
  635:         # convertible, so a pair the registry refuses reaches the job and
  636:         # comes back as the registry's own teaching message rather than a
  637:         # greyed-out control.
  638:         mode = self.vertical_mode()
  639:         if mode is VerticalMode.HORIZONTAL_AND_VERTICAL:
  640:             source_datum = self.source_vertical_datum()
  641:             target_datum = self.target_vertical_datum()
  642:             if source_datum is None or target_datum is None:
  643:                 return None
  644:         else:
  645:             source_datum = None
  646:             target_datum = None
  647: 
  648:         source = self.from_zone.currentData()
  649:         target = self.to_zone.currentData()
  650:         source_zone = source if isinstance(source, Zone) else None
  651:         target_zone = target if isinstance(target, Zone) else None
  652: 
  653:         common = dict(
  654:             input_path=None,
  655:             output_directory=None,
  656:             direction=direction,
  657:             source_zone=source_zone,
  658:             target_zone=target_zone,
  659:             input_unit=self.input_unit.currentData(),
  660:             output_unit=self.output_unit.currentData(),
  661:             # From the dropdowns (WP-V8, plan section 4.3; per-side since
  662:             # the owner's 2026-08-09 feature), exactly as MainWindow.settings
  663:             # reads its own. A grayed side emits None.
  664:             geoid_model=self.output_geoid_model(),
  665:             source_geoid_model=self.input_geoid_model(),
  666:             vertical_mode=mode,
  667:             source_vertical_datum=source_datum,
  668:             target_vertical_datum=target_datum,
  669:         )
  670: 
  671:         if direction is Direction.ZONE_TO_ZONE:
  672:             # A pure zone-to-zone job never consults the longitude convention
  673:             # (michspc.job._convert_row), so the interface does not pretend the
  674:             # user answered a question it never asked.
  675:             return JobSettings(**common, longitude_convention=None)
  676: 
  677:         convention = self.longitude_convention()
  678:         if convention is None:
  679:             return None
  680:         return JobSettings(**common, longitude_convention=convention)
  681: 
  682:     def _vertical_only_settings(self) -> JobSettings | None:
  683:         """The vertical-only job this tab's controls describe, or None.
  684: 
  685:         Mirrors ``MainWindow._vertical_only_settings`` exactly - the From
  686:         selection is the input system, ``target_zone`` is None because no
  687:         output horizontal system exists, ``output_unit`` IS the input unit
  688:         because the display mirrors the input's columns, and the longitude
  689:         convention is stated for a geodetic input and None for a zone input -
  690:         and differs in one respect only: ``input_path`` and
  691:         ``output_directory`` are None, the statement this tab always makes.
  692:         """
  693:         source = self.from_zone.currentData()
  694:         if source == UNCHOSEN:
  695:             return None
  696:         source_zone = source if isinstance(source, Zone) else None
  697: 
  698:         source_datum = self.source_vertical_datum()
  699:         target_datum = self.target_vertical_datum()
  700:         if source_datum is None or target_datum is None:
  701:             return None
  702: 
  703:         input_unit = self.input_unit.currentData()
  704:         common = dict(
  705:             input_path=None,
  706:             output_directory=None,
  707:             direction=Direction.VERTICAL_ONLY,
  708:             source_zone=source_zone,
  709:             target_zone=None,
  710:             input_unit=input_unit,
  711:             output_unit=input_unit,
  712:             geoid_model=self.output_geoid_model(),
  713:             source_geoid_model=self.input_geoid_model(),
  714:             vertical_mode=VerticalMode.VERTICAL,
  715:             source_vertical_datum=source_datum,
  716:             target_vertical_datum=target_datum,
  717:         )
  718: 
  719:         if source_zone is not None:
  720:             return JobSettings(**common, longitude_convention=None)
  721: 
  722:         convention = self.longitude_convention()
  723:         if convention is None:
  724:             return None
  725:         return JobSettings(**common, longitude_convention=convention)

  776:     def _on_direction_changed(self) -> None:
  777:         self._update_entry_labels()
  778:         self._update_unit_labels()

  780:         self._update_angle_format_relevance()
