  784:     def _on_angle_format_changed(self) -> None:
  785:         """The typed point is discarded when the format changes, deliberately.
  786: 
  787:         The two pages hold different text, and carrying a decimal 43.800 over
  788:         into a degrees box would read as 43 degrees flat - the same number
  789:         meaning a different point, 48 minutes away, with nothing said. Nothing
  790:         is translated between the pages: the abandoned page keeps whatever was
  791:         in it and stops gating Convert, and ``_update_convert_enabled`` reads
  792:         only the page now showing.
  793:         """
  794:         self._update_entry_pages()
  795:         self._update_convert_enabled()
  796:         self._invalidate_result()
  797: 
  798:     def _on_entry_changed(self) -> None:
  799:         """What every DMS box is wired to: the same pair the decimal box uses."""
  800:         self._update_convert_enabled()
  801:         self._invalidate_result()
  802: 
  803:     def _on_longitude_changed(self) -> None:
  804:         """Gating AND invalidation, like every other selection on this tab.
  805: 
  806:         The convention decides which point the typed longitude names - the two
  807:         readings are 340 miles apart - so a result computed under the old
  808:         convention no longer describes what the controls say (DESIGN.md #43,
  809:         closing the control amendment #26's fix missed).
  810:         """
  811:         self._update_convert_enabled()
  812:         self._invalidate_result()
  813: 
  814:     def _on_vertical_mode_changed(self) -> None:
  815:         """The toggle reveals or hides the datum rows - and, like every
  816:         control that changes what the job IS, discards a displayed result:
  817:         the same numbers under a different mode describe a different job
  818:         (amendment #26). The longitude selector's relevance follows the mode
  819:         too: vertical-only mode reads it from the From selection alone."""
  820:         self._update_vertical_rows()
  821:         self._update_longitude_relevance()
  822:         self._update_convert_enabled()
  823:         self._invalidate_result()
  824: 
  825:     def _on_vertical_datum_changed(self) -> None:
  826:         """What both datum combos are wired to: the geoid-side refresh (a
  827:         datum decides which models its side may offer, the per-side
  828:         registry filter), then the gate and the invalidation, the same pair
  829:         the coordinate entries use."""
  830:         self._refresh_geoid_sides()
  831:         self._update_convert_enabled()
  832:         self._invalidate_result()
  833: 
  834:     def _refresh_geoid_sides(self) -> None:
  835:         """Make both geoid combos offer what the mode and datums allow -
  836:         ``MainWindow._refresh_geoid_sides``'s rule exactly, through the same
  837:         ``controls`` helpers, so the two tabs cannot filter differently. A
  838:         side with no models is DISABLED - grayed, the owner's word - rather
  839:         than hidden or left enabled over an empty list."""
  840:         mode = self.vertical_mode()
  841:         if not mode.converts_elevations:
  842:             self.geoid_label.setText(GEOID_MODEL_LABEL)
  843:             refresh_geoid_combo(self.geoid_combo, geoid.ALL_GEOID_MODELS)
  844:             refresh_geoid_combo(
  845:                 self.input_geoid_combo,
  846:                 geoid_models_for_datum(self.source_vertical_datum()),
  847:             )
  848:             return
  849:         self.geoid_label.setText(OUTPUT_GEOID_LABEL)
  850:         refresh_geoid_combo(
  851:             self.geoid_combo,
  852:             geoid_models_for_datum(self.target_vertical_datum()),
  853:         )
  854:         refresh_geoid_combo(
  855:             self.input_geoid_combo,
  856:             geoid_models_for_datum(self.source_vertical_datum()),
  857:         )
  858: 
  859:     def _update_vertical_rows(self) -> None:
  860:         """Show the datum rows when elevations convert; hide the output
  861:         horizontal controls in vertical-only mode.
  862: 
  863:         Hidden, not disabled, in both directions of this method (plan section
  864:         4.2). The combos keep whatever answer they held, so toggling modes
  865:         does not silently discard a chosen datum or a chosen To zone - but a
  866:         job that does not consult a hidden control never reads it:
  867:         ``settings`` states None for a horizontal job's datums, and a
  868:         vertical-only job never reads the To zone or the output unit.
  869:         """
  870:         mode = self.vertical_mode()
  871:         for widget in (
  872:             self.vertical_source_label,
  873:             self.vertical_source_combo,
  874:             self.vertical_target_label,
  875:             self.vertical_target_combo,
  876:             # The input-side geoid row is vertical-mode furniture exactly
  877:             # as the datum rows it filters by are; within the vertical
  878:             # modes a side with no models is GRAYED by
  879:             # _refresh_geoid_sides, never hidden (the owner's distinction,
  880:             # 2026-08-09).
  881:             self.input_geoid_label,
  882:             self.input_geoid_combo,
  883:         ):
  884:             widget.setVisible(mode.converts_elevations)
  885:         self._refresh_geoid_sides()
  886:         vertical_only = mode is VerticalMode.VERTICAL
  887:         for widget in (
  888:             self.to_zone_label,
  889:             self.to_zone,
  890:             self.output_unit_label,
  891:             self.output_unit,
  892:         ):
  893:             widget.setVisible(not vertical_only)
  894: 
  895:     def entering_dms(self) -> bool:
  896:         """True when the two coordinate rows are showing their four-box page.
  897: 
  898:         Both conditions, not just the dropdown: a northing has no minutes, so
  899:         the DMS page is never shown while the job starts from a zone however
  900:         the selector happens to be set.
  901:         """
  902:         return (
  903:             self.from_zone.currentData() == GEODETIC
  904:             and self.angle_format.currentData() == DMS_PAGE
  905:         )
  906: 
  907:     def _update_entry_pages(self) -> None:
  908:         page = DMS_PAGE if self.entering_dms() else DECIMAL_PAGE
  909:         self.first_stack.setCurrentIndex(page)
  910:         self.second_stack.setCurrentIndex(page)
  911: 
  912:     def _update_angle_format_relevance(self) -> None:
  913:         """The selector matters only when the typed values are angles."""
  914:         relevant = self.from_zone.currentData() == GEODETIC
  915:         self.angle_format_label.setEnabled(relevant)
  916:         self.angle_format.setEnabled(relevant)
  917:         self._update_entry_pages()
  918: 
  919:     def _invalidate_result(self) -> None:
  920:         """Discard a displayed result the controls no longer describe.
  921: 
  922:         Found by the closing review gate, as a CRITICAL. Editing any field or
  923:         selection after a conversion left the previous point's answer on the
  924:         screen, still captioned "Converted", with both copy paths still live -
  925:         so a surveyor who changed a northing and did not press Convert could
  926:         copy the PREVIOUS point's coordinate straight into CAD. The reviewer's
  927:         counterexample: Michigan Central to Michigan South, N=176,200.000
  928:         converts to N=838,214.295; editing the entry to 276,200.000 without
  929:         converting left 838,214.295 on screen, while the point now in the
  930:         controls is 938,215.332 - a stale reading 100,001.037 ft out.
  931: 
  932:         Clearing rather than marking stale: a greyed-out or annotated result is
  933:         still a number on the screen next to a Copy button, and this program's
  934:         rule is that a value it cannot stand behind is not displayed at all.
  935: 
  936:         Idempotent, so the many signals that reach it cost nothing when there is
  937:         no result to discard.
  938:         """
  939:         if self.result is None and self.panel.sections is None:
  940:             return
 1025:         without one would invent a requirement the format does not have.
 1026:         """
 1027:         if self.settings() is None:
 1028:             return False
 1029:         return self.coordinates_are_typed()
 1030: 
 1031:     def _update_convert_enabled(self) -> None:
 1032:         self.convert_button.setEnabled(self.form_is_complete())
 1033: 
 1034:     # ------------------------------------------------------------------
 1035:     # Running the conversion
 1036:     # ------------------------------------------------------------------
 1037: 
 1038:     def convert(self) -> bool:
 1039:         """Convert the typed point and display it. True if it landed.
 1040: 
 1041:         **No wait cursor and no ``setEnabled(False)``.** That ceremony exists in
 1042:         ``MainWindow.convert`` because a file of several thousand points takes
 1043:         long enough for a frozen window to look like a crash. One point is
 1044:         instantaneous - a single ``_convert_row`` and one geoid lookup - so the
 1045:         same ceremony here would be a flicker that says "this is slow" about
 1046:         something that is not. Recorded as a decision rather than an omission.
 1047: 
 1048:         Nothing is written on this path at all, so there is no overwrite prompt
 1049:         and no output folder to open.
 1050:         """
 1051:         settings = self.settings()
 1052:         if settings is None or not self.form_is_complete():
 1053:             # Convert is disabled in this state; reaching here would mean the
 1054:             # enablement logic and this method disagree, so say so rather than
 1055:             # converting a half-specified point.
 1056:             self._report_failure(ValueError(INCOMPLETE_FORM))
 1057:             return False
 1058: 
 1059:         self.result = None
 1060:         self.last_failure = None
 1061:         self._render_sections(None)
 1062: 
 1063:         try:
 1064:             first, second = self.typed_coordinates(settings)
 1065:             parsed = pnezd.parse_typed_point(
 1066:                 first,
 1067:                 second,
 1068:                 self.elevation_edit.text(),
 1069:                 source=self.typed_point_source(),
 1070:             )
 1071:             result = run(settings, source=parsed)
 1072:             sections = single_point_sections(result)
 1073:             warnings = single_point_warnings(result)
 1074:         except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - shown in full, then stopped
 1075:             self._report_failure(error)
 1076:             return False
 1077: 
 1078:         self.result = result
 1079:         self._render_sections(sections, warnings)
 1080:         self._report_success(result)
 1081:         return True
 1082: 
 1083:     # ------------------------------------------------------------------
 1084:     # The results panel
 1085:     # ------------------------------------------------------------------
 1086: 
 1087:     def _render_sections(
 1088:         self,
 1089:         sections: tuple[ResultSection, ...] | None,
 1090:         warnings: str = NO_RESULT_WARNINGS,
 1091:     ) -> None:
 1092:         """Show a result, or ``None`` to empty the panel.
 1093: 
 1094:         Copy all follows the panel exactly: it is enabled when, and only when,
 1095:         there is something on screen to copy.
 1096: 
 1097:         The warnings field is driven from here rather than from ``convert``, so
 1098:         that it is emptied by the SAME call that empties the panel. Every route
 1099:         that discards a result - a control changing, a refusal, a fresh run
 1100:         starting - goes through this method, and a warnings field that outlived
 1101:         the numbers it described would be the stale-result failure of amendment
 1102:         #26 in a new place: the surveyor would read "none" beside a blank panel
 1103:         and take it as this point's answer.
 1104:         """
 1105:         self.panel.render_sections(sections)
 1106:         self.copy_all_button.setEnabled(sections is not None)
 1107:         self.warnings_label.setText(warnings)
 1108: 
 1109:     @property
 1110:     def sections(self) -> tuple[ResultSection, ...] | None:
 1111:         """The exact sections the panel last rendered, or None if it is empty.
 1112: 
 1113:         Read from the panel rather than kept beside it, so Copy all serialises
 1114:         what is on screen rather than a second copy of it that could go stale.
 1115:         """
RESULTS
   70:     """Whether the table's coordinate columns hold degrees for this job.
   71: 
   72:     True for a State-Plane-to-geodetic job, and for a vertical-only job whose
   73:     input is geodetic: that job's cells reproduce the input positions, so a
   74:     geodetic input keeps geodetic headings. The same branch
   75:     ``exports._geodetic_coordinate_columns`` takes for the files, restated
   76:     here only because this module may not import the file layer's private
   77:     names; ``row_strings`` renders through the identical formatters either
   78:     way, so the two cannot show different digits.
   79:     """
   80:     return settings.direction is Direction.ZONE_TO_GEODETIC or (
   81:         settings.direction is Direction.VERTICAL_ONLY
   82:         and settings.source_zone is None
   83:     )
   84: 
   85: 
   86: def _elevation_heading(settings, geoid_model_name: str | None) -> str:
   87:     """``Elevation (NAVD88, m)`` - the ordinary heading with the TARGET datum
   88:     and the OUTPUT unit.
   89: 
   90:     Vertical jobs only. The table's elevation cells hold the SHIFTED height
   91:     (``row_strings`` renders ``point.output_elevation``, which _convert_row
   92:     moved into the target datum), so a bare "Elevation" over them names the
   93:     number without saying which surface it is on - the WP-V7 review gate's
   94:     finding 4, assigned to WP-V8. The unit is the OUTPUT unit - the unit the
   95:     cells beneath are actually rendered in (``row_strings`` formats them with
   96:     ``settings.output_unit``), NOT the input unit that governs the shift and
   97:     sigma columns beside it. The datum and unit come from the settings the
   98:     job actually ran with, never from a dropdown's current state.
   99: 
  100:     Delegates to ``_datum_elevation_label`` rather than repeating its
  101:     template. The two were separate f-strings producing the same text until
  102:     the geoid tag arrived (DESIGN.md #52), which is one fact in two places -
  103:     exactly the arrangement that lets a panel and a table drift apart, and
  104:     the property amendment #26 spent a whole gate establishing is that they
  105:     cannot.
  106:     """
  107:     return _datum_elevation_label(
  108:         settings.target_vertical_datum, settings.output_unit, geoid_model_name
  109:     )
  110: 
  111: 
  112: def table_geoid_model_name(result: JobResult | None) -> str | None:
  113:     """The geoid model the table's elevation cells are ON, or None.
  114: 
  115:     Named only for a geoid-to-geoid job, where the output elevation's identity
  116:     is the model and nothing else: both ends carry the same datum and the same
  117:     unit, so without it the heading describes the input column equally well
  118:     (the owner's instruction, 2026-08-10). Every other job leaves it None -
  119:     a leveled NAVD 88 height does not belong to a geoid model (DESIGN.md #50),
  120:     and a horizontal job asked no vertical question at all.
  121: 
  122:     The transformation is resolved through the same registry lookup ``job.run``
  123:     and the record perform, and the swap decision through ``job.geoid_swap_models``,
  124:     so the heading cannot claim a swap the computation did not make.
  125:     """
  126:     if result is None:
  127:         return None
  128:     settings = result.settings
  129:     if not settings.vertical_mode.converts_elevations:
  130:         return None
  131:     swap = geoid_swap_models(
  132:         settings,
  133:         require_vertical_pair(
  134:             settings.source_vertical_datum, settings.target_vertical_datum
  135:         ),
  136:     )
  137:     return None if swap is None else swap[1].name
  138: 
  139: 
  140: def columns_for(result: JobResult | None) -> tuple[str, ...]:
  141:     """The header row for a job's direction. ``COLUMNS`` until a job says else.
  142: 
  143:     An empty table shows the ordinary headings: nothing has been converted, so
  144:     naming the columns after a direction the user has not run would be the
  145:     interface answering a question it was not asked.
  146: 
  147:     A HORIZONTAL_AND_VERTICAL job renames the Elevation heading with the
  148:     target datum and gains the shift and sigma columns directly after it -
  149:     mirroring ``exports.audit_columns``, whose vertical block also sits
  150:     directly after Elevation, so the table and the audit CSV read in the same
  151:     order. A horizontal job's header is ``COLUMNS`` (or ``GEODETIC_COLUMNS``)
  152:     unchanged, to the string: horizontal mode asked no vertical question and
  153:     its table must not claim a datum nobody stated (plan section 1).
  154:     """
  155:     if result is None:
  156:         return COLUMNS
  157:     settings = result.settings
  158:     columns = list(
  159:         GEODETIC_COLUMNS if _geodetic_display_columns(settings) else COLUMNS
  160:     )
  161:     if settings.vertical_mode.converts_elevations:
  162:         at = columns.index("Elevation")
  163:         columns[at] = _elevation_heading(settings, table_geoid_model_name(result))
  164:         # The shift and sigma headings carry the INPUT unit - the unit the
  165:         # elevations were supplied in, the owner's instruction (2026-08-09).
  166:         # In vertical-only mode input and output units are equal by
  167:         # construction; in Horizontal + Vertical they can differ, and the
  168:         # input unit still governs these two columns while the Elevation
  169:         # heading beside them names the output unit its own cells are in.
  170:         # ``row_strings`` converts the cells with the same unit object.
  171:         columns[at + 1 : at + 1] = [
  172:             vertical_shift_heading(settings.input_unit),
  173:             vertical_sigma_heading(settings.input_unit),
  174:         ]
  175:     return tuple(columns)
  176: 
  177: POINT_COLUMN = 0
  178: NORTHING_COLUMN = 1
  179: EASTING_COLUMN = 2
  180: ELEVATION_COLUMN = 3
  240:             northing = fmt.latitude(point.output_northing)
  241:             easting = fmt.longitude(point.output_easting)
  242:         else:
  243:             northing = fmt.coordinate(point.output_northing, settings.output_unit)
  244:             easting = fmt.coordinate(point.output_easting, settings.output_unit)
  245: 
  246:         cells = [
  247:             point.point_id,
  248:             northing,
  249:             easting,
  250:             fmt.coordinate(point.output_elevation, settings.output_unit),
  251:             fmt.factor(point.factors.grid_scale_factor),
  252:             # combined_factor is None for a point with no usable elevation;
  253:             # fmt.factor renders that as "N/A", never as 1.0.
  254:             fmt.factor(point.factors.combined_factor),
  255:             "; ".join(warning.code.value for warning in point.warnings),
  256:         ]
  257: 
  258:         if vertical:
  259:             # Directly after the Elevation cell, under the two headings
  260:             # columns_for inserts at the same position. WHICH numbers these
  261:             # two cells carry is exports.vertical_shift_and_sigma_m's one
  262:             # statement, shared with the audit CSV, so this cell and the
  263:             # CSV's cannot disagree (#26's property): the datum reading's
  264:             # shift and sigma ordinarily, the SWAP shift with no sigma on a
  265:             # geoid-to-geoid point, neither where no reading exists (no
  266:             # elevation, or a coverage refusal - the warnings cell says
  267:             # which). A None renders N/A - never the raw figure, which is
  268:             # not an uncertainty (DESIGN.md #36). Both are converted into
  269:             # the INPUT unit - the same unit object ``columns_for`` built
  270:             # the two headings from (and the same one ``exports.audit_rows``
  271:             # converts its cells with), so heading and value cannot claim
  272:             # different units.
  273:             shift_m, sigma_m = vertical_shift_and_sigma_m(point)
  274:             cells[ELEVATION_COLUMN + 1 : ELEVATION_COLUMN + 1] = [
  275:                 fmt.vertical_quantity(shift_m, settings.input_unit),
  276:                 fmt.vertical_quantity(sigma_m, settings.input_unit),
  277:             ]
  278: 
  279:         rows.append(tuple(cells))
  280: 
  281:     return tuple(rows)
  282: 
  283: 
  284: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------
  285: # The single-point results display (docs/DESIGN.md amendment #26).
  286: #
  287: # Pure functions of a JobResult, and widgets are built from what they return.
  288: # Nothing below touches Qt, which is what lets the layout the owner approved be
  289: # asserted label by label without a QApplication - and what keeps the layout a
  290: # statement about the conversion rather than a property of some widget tree.
  380:     what moved the INPUT elevation to the OUTPUT one, and a bare "Shift" copied
  381:     into a spreadsheet says nothing about which way. ``unit`` is the job's
  382:     input unit (the owner's instruction, 2026-08-09), the same one the value
  383:     beside this label is converted with.
  384:     """
  385:     return (
  386:         f"Vertical shift {transformation.source.code} -> "
  387:         f"{transformation.target.code} ({unit.code})"
  388:     )
  389: 
  390: 
  391: def geoid_swap_label(swap, unit) -> str:
  392:     """``Geoid change GEOID12B -> GEOID18 (ift)`` - a geoid-to-geoid point's
  393:     shift row label: the MODELS, not the datums.
  394: 
  395:     On a swap job the datum did not move - the transformation is an identity
  396:     - so a label naming the datums would describe the one thing that did not
  397:     happen. What moved the elevation is the change of geoid model, and the
  398:     label says so in the shape ``vertical_shift_label`` established: what
  399:     changed, which way, and the unit the value beside it is rendered in
  400:     (the job's input unit, the owner's 2026-08-09 instruction). The audit
  401:     CSV's generic ``Vertical shift (unit)`` / ``Shift sigma (unit)``
  402:     headings stay - they are true of both kinds of shift - so #17's
  403:     one-wording rule is not in play where the surfaces differ on purpose.
  404:     """
  405:     return (
  406:         f"Geoid change {swap.source_model_name} -> "
  407:         f"{swap.target_model_name} ({unit.code})"
  408:     )
  409: 
  410: 
  411: def _datum_elevation_label(datum, unit, geoid_model_name: str | None = None) -> str:
  412:     """``Elevation (NAVD88, m)``, or ``Elevation (NAVD88, m) (GEOID18)`` on a
  413:     geoid-to-geoid job - the datum, the unit, and the geoid where one applies.
  414: 
  415:     THE one elevation-label template. The Multi point table heading builds on
  416:     it too (``_elevation_heading``), so the two surfaces cannot word the same
  417:     fact differently.
  418: 
  419:     Only a vertical conversion uses this: a horizontal job asked no vertical
  420:     question and its rows must not claim a datum nobody stated (plan section
  421:     1, the owner's decision that horizontal mode is unchanged) - which is
  422:     also why a horizontal row's plain "Elevation" gains no unit here: the
  423:     Units row already serves it, and nothing about horizontal output may
  424:     change. ``unit`` is the unit the row's VALUE is rendered in - the input
  425:     unit for the INPUT section's elevation, the output unit for the OUTPUT
  426:     section's - passed by the caller that formats the value, so label and
  427:     value cannot name different units.
  428: 
  429:     ``geoid_model_name`` is the owner's instruction of 2026-08-10, and it is
  430:     a SEPARATE parenthesis after the unit one, in his words. Supplied only on
  431:     a geoid-to-geoid job, where the two elevations sit on the same datum in
  432:     the same unit and the model is the whole difference between them: without
  433:     it the INPUT and OUTPUT labels are the same string over two different
  434:     heights. Left None everywhere else, because an ordinary orthometric
  435:     height is not "in" a geoid model - the hybrid models were fitted to the
  436:     leveled network, and naming one beside a leveled height would state a
  437:     dependence that does not exist (DESIGN.md #50's recorded geodetic fact).
  438:     """
  439:     label = f"{ELEVATION_LABEL} ({datum.code}, {unit.code})"
  440:     if geoid_model_name is None:
  441:         return label
  442:     return f"{label} ({geoid_model_name})"
  443: 
  444: 
  445: def _vertical_rows(reading, swap, unit) -> tuple[ResultValue, ...]:
  446:     """The shift row and the sigma row, or nothing at all.
  447: 
  448:     Empty when the point carries no ``VerticalReading`` - a horizontal job, a
  449:     vertical point with no elevation, or a coverage-refused point whose
  450:     warning says why. ``unit`` is the job's INPUT unit - the unit both labels
  451:     name and both values are converted into (the owner's instruction,
  452:     2026-08-09). The sigma renders through ``fmt.vertical_quantity``, which
  453:     prints N/A for a None - the ONLY thing an unavailable sigma may print
  454:     (docs/DESIGN.md #36); the reason it is unavailable reaches the warnings
  455:     field through ``WarningCode.VERTICAL_SIGMA_UNAVAILABLE``, raised beside
  456:     the reading by ``job._convert_row``.
  457: 
  458:     On a geoid-to-geoid point (``swap`` is the point's ``GeoidSwapReading``)
  459:     the shift row is the GEOID CHANGE - the value that actually moved the
  460:     elevation, labelled with the two models - and the sigma row is N/A with
  461:     nothing attached: NGS publishes no error model for the difference
  462:     between two of its geoid models, so no number exists to print.
  463:     """
  464:     if reading is None:
  465:         return ()
  466:     if swap is not None:
  467:         return (
  468:             ResultValue(
  469:                 geoid_swap_label(swap, unit),
  470:                 fmt.vertical_quantity(swap.shift_m, unit),
  471:             ),
  472:             ResultValue(
  473:                 vertical_sigma_heading(unit),
  474:                 fmt.vertical_quantity(None, unit),
  475:             ),
  476:         )
  477:     return (
  478:         ResultValue(
  479:             vertical_shift_label(reading.transformation, unit),
  480:             fmt.vertical_quantity(reading.shift_m, unit),
  481:         ),
  482:         ResultValue(
  483:             vertical_sigma_heading(unit),
  484:             fmt.vertical_quantity(reading.sigma_m, unit),
  485:         ),
  650:             f"job carries {len(result.points)}. Refused rather than showing the "
  651:             f"first of them, which would name one point's coordinates as though "
  652:             f"they were the whole job's."
  653:         )
  654: 
  655:     settings = result.settings
  656:     point = result.points[0]
  657:     conversion = point.conversion
  658:     factors = point.factors
  659:     positive_west = _positive_west(settings)
  660: 
  661:     # The vertical rows (WP-V7, plan section 5.2). Gated on the point CARRYING
  662:     # a VerticalReading, not on the settings: a horizontal job's layout is
  663:     # unchanged to the label, and a vertical point whose shift was refused
  664:     # (coverage) or that had no elevation shows no shift row - its warning, in
  665:     # the warnings field, is what explains the absence. When a reading exists,
  666:     # the INPUT elevation is labelled with the source datum and the OUTPUT one
  667:     # with the target datum, because the two rows now hold heights on two
  668:     # different surfaces and unlabelled they would read as the same quantity
  669:     # re-expressed. Each label carries the unit its own VALUE is rendered in
  670:     # - the input unit for the INPUT row, the output unit for the OUTPUT row
  671:     # (they can differ in a Horizontal + Vertical job): symmetric on purpose,
  672:     # because a unit on the output row alone would invite "in what?" of the
  673:     # input one. The shift and sigma rows carry the INPUT unit (the owner's
  674:     # instruction, 2026-08-09).
  675:     #
  676:     # On a geoid-to-geoid point each label also names ITS OWN side's model -
  677:     # input GEOID12B, output GEOID18 (the owner's instruction, 2026-08-10) -
  678:     # because there the datum and the unit are identical at both ends and the
  679:     # model is the only thing telling the two heights apart. The names come
  680:     # from the point's own ``GeoidSwapReading``, not from the settings: a
  681:     # point whose swap was refused (its grids would not read - the
  682:     # GEOID_SWAP_UNAVAILABLE warning) carries no reading and gets no model
  683:     # name, which is the truth about that point even in a job where every
  684:     # other point converted.
  685:     reading = point.vertical
  686:     swap = point.geoid_swap
  687:     if reading is not None:
  688:         input_elevation_label = _datum_elevation_label(
  689:             reading.transformation.source,
  690:             settings.input_unit,
  691:             None if swap is None else swap.source_model_name,
  692:         )
  693:         output_elevation_label = _datum_elevation_label(
  694:             reading.transformation.target,
  695:             settings.output_unit,
  696:             None if swap is None else swap.target_model_name,
  697:         )
  698:     else:
  699:         input_elevation_label = ELEVATION_LABEL
  700:         output_elevation_label = ELEVATION_LABEL
  701:     vertical_rows = _vertical_rows(reading, swap, settings.input_unit)
  702: 
  703:     if settings.direction is Direction.VERTICAL_ONLY:
  704:         # The vertical-only layouts (the owner's feature, 2026-08-09). The
  705:         # INPUT section describes the typed point in its own system, with the
  706:         # factors - the input zone's where one exists, honestly absent where
  707:         # none does. The OUTPUT section holds ONLY the target-datum elevation,
  708:         # the shift and the sigma: nothing was converted horizontally, and
  709:         # the unchanged coordinates repeated under an OUTPUT heading would
  710:         # read as a conversion that never ran.
  711:         if settings.source_zone is not None:
  712:             source = ResultSection(
  713:                 INPUT_TITLE,
  714:                 (
  715:                     *_grid_values(
  716:                         settings.source_zone,
  717:                         settings.input_unit,
  718:                         point.row.northing,
  719:                         point.row.easting,
  720:                         point.row.elevation,
  721:                         elevation_label=input_elevation_label,
  722:                     ),
  723:                     # The INPUT zone's factors, the ZONE_TO_GEODETIC
  724:                     # precedent: no output zone exists, so every factor
  725:                     # describes the typed State Plane point.
  726:                     ResultValue(
  727:                         GRID_FACTOR_LABEL, fmt.factor(factors.grid_scale_factor)
  728:                     ),
  729:                     ResultValue(
  730:                         CONVERGENCE_LABEL,
  900:     Separate from ``single_point_sections`` because the owner put warnings in a
  901:     field of their own (docs/DESIGN.md amendment #30) - but built from the same
  902:     ``_warnings_text`` the sections used to call, so moving the display did not
  903:     create a second account of what a warning says.
  904: 
  905:     Refuses a multi-point job for the same reason ``single_point_sections``
  906:     does: naming one point's warnings as though they were the whole job's is
  907:     exactly the kind of quiet mis-statement this program refuses.
  908:     """
  909:     if len(result.points) != 1:
  910:         raise ValueError(
  911:             f"The single-point warnings field describes one converted point "
  912:             f"and this job carries {len(result.points)}. Refused rather than "
  913:             f"showing the first of them."
  914:         )
  915:     return _warnings_text(result.points[0])
  916: 
  917: 
  918: def single_point_clipboard_text(sections: tuple[ResultSection, ...]) -> str:
  919:     """The sections as tab-separated text, for the Copy all button.
  920: 
  921:     Section title on its own line, then one ``label<TAB>value`` line per value,
  922:     with a blank line between sections and none at the end. Tabs because the
  923:     result is pasted into a spreadsheet or an email as often as into a text
  924:     file, and a tab is the one separator that survives all three - and because
  925:     a comma would collide with the very thousands separators this program
  926:     argues about elsewhere.
  927: 
  928:     Warnings are not in these sections and so are not in this text, which is
  929:     the owner's instruction (amendment #30): the clipboard carries the numbers
  930:     a surveyor pastes into CAD or a spreadsheet, and a paragraph of prose
  931:     dropped among them has to be deleted there. The warnings field on screen is
  932:     where they are read.
  933:     """
  934:     blocks = []
  935:     for section in sections:
  936:         lines = [section.title]
  937:         lines.extend(f"{value.label}\t{value.text}" for value in section.values)
  938:         blocks.append("\n".join(lines))
  939:     return "\n\n".join(blocks)
  940: 
  941:

  943:     """A read-only table of already-formatted strings.
  944: 
  945:     Read-only on purpose: core result records are frozen and the interface never

   22: shipped grids' own difference at that position is -0.032343 m - inside
   23: 0.0015 m of the fixture figure, the bound two 0.5 mm print quantizations
   24: plus the reader's measured sub-millimetre residual allow - and the job's
   25: swap shift is pinned EQUAL to that grid difference exactly, because the swap
   26: reads the very grids ``geoid.default_grid`` serves.
   27: 
   28: The other half of the feature is what did NOT change: every pre-existing job
   29: shape - horizontal, and every #41-era vertical shape - states
   30: ``source_geoid_model=None`` and keeps every byte of every surface, and the
   31: #41 either-endpoint behaviour (NAVD88 -> NGVD29 with GEOID18 in
   32: ``geoid_model``) is reproduced exactly by the normalization that supersedes
   33: it (``job.per_side_geoid_models``).
   34: 
   35: No Codex gate ran over this feature (the owner's instruction): these
   36: hand-derived pins and the falsification table in the session record carry
   37: the weight instead.
   38: """
   39: 
   40: from __future__ import annotations
   41: 
   42: import csv
   43: import os
   44: 
   45: # MUST precede any Qt import: ``results_model`` imports PySide6 at module
   46: # level, and the platform plugin is chosen at import time
   47: # (docs/method/TOOLING.md).
   48: os.environ["QT_QPA_PLATFORM"] = "offscreen"
   49: 
   50: import pytest  # noqa: E402
   51: 
   52: from michspc.fileio import exports, formatting as fmt, geoid, pnezd  # noqa: E402
   53: from michspc.fileio.report import build_report  # noqa: E402
   54: from michspc.gui import results_model  # noqa: E402
   55: from michspc.job import (
   56:     Direction,
   57:     GeoidSwapReading,
   58:     JobSettings,
   59:     LongitudeConvention,
   60:     VerticalMode,
   61:     factors_use_source_era,
   62:     geoid_swap_models,
   63:     per_side_geoid_models,
   64:     run,
   65: )
   66: from michspc.spc.convert import WarningCode
   67: from michspc.spc.units import INTERNATIONAL_FEET, METERS
   68: from michspc.spc.vertical import NAVD88, NGVD29, require_vertical_pair
   69: from tests.fixtures.geoid12b_anchors import GEOID12B_ANCHORS
   70: from tests.fixtures.geoid_anchors import GEOID_ANCHORS
   71: 
   72: # The Houghton anchor position, present in BOTH fixture sets at the same
   73: # coordinates - resolved from the fixtures rather than retyped, so this file
   74: # cannot drift from the captured NGS truth.
   75: HOUGHTON_LATITUDE = 47.1211
   76: HOUGHTON_LONGITUDE = -88.5694
   77: N18_FIXTURE = next(
   78:     a.geoid_height_m
   79:     for a in GEOID_ANCHORS
   80:     if a.latitude == HOUGHTON_LATITUDE and a.longitude == HOUGHTON_LONGITUDE
   81: )
   82: N12B_FIXTURE = next(
   83:     a.geoid_height_m
   84:     for a in GEOID12B_ANCHORS
   85:     if a.latitude == HOUGHTON_LATITUDE and a.longitude == HOUGHTON_LONGITUDE
   86: )
   87: 
   88: # -0.032 m: the 12B -> 18 swap shift as NGS's printed figures give it.
   89: FIXTURE_SWAP_SHIFT_M = N12B_FIXTURE - N18_FIXTURE
   90: 
   91: # Two NGS figures each printed to 0.001 m carry +/-0.0005 m of quantization
   92: # apiece, and the reader's worst measured residual against NGS's service is
   93: # sub-millimetre (DESIGN.md #37), so 0.0015 m is the derived bound - not a
   94: # chosen one.
   95: SWAP_TOLERANCE_M = 0.0015
   96: 
   97: 
   98: def _swap_source(
   99:     elevation: str = "200.000", point_id: str = "101"
  100: ) -> pnezd.PnezdFile:
  101:     return pnezd.parse_lines(
  102:         [
  103:             f"{point_id},{HOUGHTON_LATITUDE},{HOUGHTON_LONGITUDE},"
  104:             f"{elevation},SWAP"
  105:         ]
  106:     )
  107: 
  108: 
  109: def _swap_settings(**overrides) -> JobSettings:
  110:     """A geodetic-input vertical-only GEOID12B -> GEOID18 swap, metres:
  111:     identity datum pair NAVD 88 -> NAVD 88, input model GEOID12B, output
  112:     model GEOID18."""
  113:     base = dict(
  114:         input_path=None,
  115:         output_directory=None,
  116:         direction=Direction.VERTICAL_ONLY,
  117:         source_zone=None,
  118:         target_zone=None,
  119:         input_unit=METERS,
  120:         output_unit=METERS,
  121:         longitude_convention=LongitudeConvention.NEGATIVE_WEST,
  122:         vertical_mode=VerticalMode.VERTICAL,
  123:         source_vertical_datum=NAVD88,
  124:         target_vertical_datum=NAVD88,
  125:         geoid_model=geoid.GEOID18_MODEL,
  126:         source_geoid_model=geoid.GEOID12B_MODEL,
  127:     )
  128:     base.update(overrides)
  129:     return JobSettings(**base)
  130: 
  131: 
  132: def _grid_difference_m(source_model, target_model) -> float:
  133:     """N_in - N_out at Houghton from the shipped grids themselves - the very
  134:     lookups the job performs, so the pin below is exact, not approximate."""
  135:     n_in = geoid.geoid_height(
  136:         HOUGHTON_LATITUDE, HOUGHTON_LONGITUDE, geoid.default_grid(source_model)
  137:     )
  138:     n_out = geoid.geoid_height(
  139:         HOUGHTON_LATITUDE, HOUGHTON_LONGITUDE, geoid.default_grid(target_model)
  140:     )
  141:     return n_in - n_out
  142: 
  143: 
  144: # ==========================================================================
  145: # The Houghton pins: both directions, exact against our grids AND within the
  146: # fixture-derived bound of NGS's printed figures.
  147: # ==========================================================================
  148: 
  149: 
  150: def test_the_12b_to_18_swap_moves_200_to_199_968_at_houghton():
  151:     """The forward pin. 200.000 m stated against GEOID12B becomes 199.968 m
  152:     stated against GEOID18 (fixture arithmetic: -33.828 - (-33.796) =
  153:     -0.032), and the job's swap shift EQUALS the shipped grids' own
  154:     difference at the point - a swap that read the same grid twice, or
  155:     flipped the subtraction, cannot satisfy both."""
  156:     result = run(_swap_settings(), source=_swap_source())
  157:     point = result.points[0]
  158:     swap = point.geoid_swap
  159: 
  160:     assert swap is not None
  161:     assert swap.source_model_name == "GEOID12B"
  162:     assert swap.target_model_name == "GEOID18"
  163: 
  164:     # Exact against the grids the job read.
  165:     expected_shift = _grid_difference_m(
  166:         geoid.GEOID12B_MODEL, geoid.GEOID18_MODEL
  167:     )
  168:     assert swap.shift_m == expected_shift
  169:     assert point.output_elevation == 200.0 + expected_shift
  170: 
  171:     # And within the derived bound of the fixture-quantized -0.032.
  172:     assert swap.shift_m == pytest.approx(
  173:         FIXTURE_SWAP_SHIFT_M, abs=SWAP_TOLERANCE_M
  174:     )
  175:     assert point.output_elevation == pytest.approx(
  176:         200.0 + FIXTURE_SWAP_SHIFT_M, abs=SWAP_TOLERANCE_M
  177:     )
  178:     # 199.968, to the written precision.
  179:     assert fmt.coordinate(point.output_elevation, METERS) == "199.9677"
  180: 
  181:     # The two separations on the reading are the grids' own values, and the
  182:     # record's arithmetic holds by construction.
  183:     assert swap.n_source_m == geoid.geoid_height(
  184:         HOUGHTON_LATITUDE,
  185:         HOUGHTON_LONGITUDE,
  186:         geoid.default_grid(geoid.GEOID12B_MODEL),
  187:     )
  188:     assert swap.n_target_m == geoid.geoid_height(
  189:         HOUGHTON_LATITUDE,
  190:         HOUGHTON_LONGITUDE,
  191:         geoid.default_grid(geoid.GEOID18_MODEL),
  192:     )
  193: 
  194:     # The datum reading stays the identity record - both statements are
  195:     # true: the datum did not change, the geoid did.
  196:     assert point.vertical is not None
  197:     assert point.vertical.transformation.is_identity
  198:     assert point.vertical.shift_m == 0.0
  199:     assert point.vertical.sigma_m is None
  200: 
  201: 
  202: def test_the_reverse_swap_moves_200_to_200_032_at_houghton():
  203:     """The 18 -> 12B direction: the same two grids, the subtraction the
  204:     other way round, landing at 200.032. A sign convention error passes one
  205:     direction and fails the other, which is what this pair of tests buys."""
  206:     result = run(
  207:         _swap_settings(
  208:             geoid_model=geoid.GEOID12B_MODEL,
  209:             source_geoid_model=geoid.GEOID18_MODEL,
  210:         ),
  211:         source=_swap_source(),
  212:     )
  213:     point = result.points[0]
  214:     swap = point.geoid_swap
  215: 
  216:     assert swap is not None
  217:     assert swap.source_model_name == "GEOID18"
  218:     assert swap.target_model_name == "GEOID12B"
  219:     expected_shift = _grid_difference_m(
  220:         geoid.GEOID18_MODEL, geoid.GEOID12B_MODEL
  221:     )
  222:     assert swap.shift_m == expected_shift
  223:     assert point.output_elevation == 200.0 + expected_shift
  224:     assert point.output_elevation == pytest.approx(
  225:         200.0 - FIXTURE_SWAP_SHIFT_M, abs=SWAP_TOLERANCE_M
  226:     )
  227:     assert fmt.coordinate(point.output_elevation, METERS) == "200.0323"
  228: 
  229: 
  230: def test_the_two_directions_round_trip_exactly():
  231:     """One pair of grids, one subtraction, two directions: the forward and
  232:     reverse swap shifts are exact negations, so a height swapped there and
  233:     back reproduces bit-for-bit (the sums are the same two floats)."""
  234:     forward = run(_swap_settings(), source=_swap_source()).points[0]
  235:     reverse = run(
  236:         _swap_settings(
  237:             geoid_model=geoid.GEOID12B_MODEL,
  238:             source_geoid_model=geoid.GEOID18_MODEL,
  239:         ),
  240:         source=_swap_source(),
  241:     ).points[0]
  242:     assert forward.geoid_swap.shift_m == -reverse.geoid_swap.shift_m
  243: 
  244: 
  245: def test_the_swap_runs_in_horizontal_and_vertical_mode_too():
  246:     """The swap is a property of the identity pair with two models, not of
  247:     the vertical-only direction: a GEODETIC_TO_ZONE job in
  248:     HORIZONTAL_AND_VERTICAL mode swaps identically - the same pivot, the
  249:     same grids, the same shift bitwise."""
  250:     from michspc.spc.zones import MI_NORTH
  251: 
  252:     vertical_only = run(_swap_settings(), source=_swap_source()).points[0]
  253:     with_zone = run(
  254:         _swap_settings(
  255:             direction=Direction.GEODETIC_TO_ZONE,
  256:             target_zone=MI_NORTH,
  257:             vertical_mode=VerticalMode.HORIZONTAL_AND_VERTICAL,
  258:         ),
  259:         source=_swap_source(),
  260:     ).points[0]
  261: 
  262:     assert with_zone.geoid_swap is not None
  263:     assert with_zone.geoid_swap.shift_m == vertical_only.geoid_swap.shift_m
  264:     assert with_zone.output_elevation == vertical_only.output_elevation
  265:     # And this direction has a zone, so the factors exist - computed from
  266:     # the OUTPUT side: the post-swap height with the output model's grid.
  267:     assert with_zone.factors.elevation_factor is not None
  268: 
  269: 
  270: def test_the_factors_come_from_the_output_side_of_a_swap():
  271:     """Output side preferred: the factors' geoid height is the OUTPUT
  272:     model's separation, and the ellipsoid height they use equals
  273:     H_in + N_in - the held-fixed quantity - because
  274:     (H_in + N_in - N_out) + N_out = H_in + N_in."""
  275:     from michspc.spc.zones import MI_NORTH
  276: 
  277:     point = run(
  278:         _swap_settings(
  279:             direction=Direction.GEODETIC_TO_ZONE,
  280:             target_zone=MI_NORTH,
  281:             vertical_mode=VerticalMode.HORIZONTAL_AND_VERTICAL,
  282:         ),
  283:         source=_swap_source(),
  284:     ).points[0]
  285:     swap = point.geoid_swap
  286: 
  287:     assert point.factors.geoid_height == swap.n_target_m
  288:     assert point.factors.ellipsoid_height == pytest.approx(
  289:         200.0 + swap.n_source_m, abs=1e-9
  290:     )
  291:     # result.geoid_model names the factors' model: the output side.
  292:     assert point.factors.geoid_height == geoid.geoid_height(
  293:         HOUGHTON_LATITUDE,
  294:         HOUGHTON_LONGITUDE,
  295:         geoid.default_grid(geoid.GEOID18_MODEL),
  296:     )
  297: 
  298: 
  299: # ==========================================================================
  300: # The #47 rule: the shift displays in the job's INPUT unit.
  301: # ==========================================================================
  302: 
  303: 
  304: def test_the_swap_shift_displays_in_international_feet():
  305:     """An ift job's swap shift renders in ift at the unit's own 3 places.
  306: 
  307:     Hand-derivation: the grids' difference at Houghton is -0.032343 m
  308:     (pinned exact above), and -0.032343 / 0.3048 = -0.106112 -> "-0.106".
  309:     (The fixture-quantized -0.032 alone would give 0.032/0.3048 = 0.10499
  310:     -> "-0.105"; the shipped grids' extra 0.3 mm - inside the fixtures'
  311:     0.5 mm print quantization - carries the third decimal to -0.106, which
  312:     is why the pin is stated against the reader's own exact value and the
  313:     literal is derived from IT.)"""
  314:     result = run(
  315:         _swap_settings(
  316:             input_unit=INTERNATIONAL_FEET, output_unit=INTERNATIONAL_FEET
  317:         ),
  318:         source=_swap_source(elevation="656.168"),
  319:     )
  320:     point = result.points[0]
  321:     swap = point.geoid_swap
  322: 
  323:     rendered = fmt.vertical_quantity(swap.shift_m, INTERNATIONAL_FEET)
  324:     assert rendered == "-0.106"
  325:     # And the metre value underneath is still the grids' own difference -
  326:     # the internals stay metres, only the presentation converts (#47).
  327:     assert swap.shift_m == _grid_difference_m(
  328:         geoid.GEOID12B_MODEL, geoid.GEOID18_MODEL
  329:     )
  330: 
  331: 
  332: # ==========================================================================
  333: # Same model on both sides: exactly the pre-feature identity job.
  334: # ==========================================================================
  335: 
  336: 
  337: def test_same_model_both_sides_is_bitwise_the_prefeature_identity_job():
  338:     """GEOID18 stated on both sides (what the GUI emits when nothing is
  339:     changed) is NO swap: no step runs, no record exists, and every output
  340:     is bit-identical to the pre-feature shape that stated no input side at
  341:     all."""
  342:     prefeature = run(
  343:         _swap_settings(source_geoid_model=None), source=_swap_source()
  344:     ).points[0]
  345:     both_sides = run(
  346:         _swap_settings(source_geoid_model=geoid.GEOID18_MODEL),
  347:         source=_swap_source(),
  348:     ).points[0]
  349: 
  350:     assert both_sides.geoid_swap is None
  351:     assert prefeature.geoid_swap is None
  352:     assert both_sides.output_elevation == prefeature.output_elevation
  353:     assert both_sides.output_elevation == 200.0
  354:     assert both_sides.factors == prefeature.factors
  355:     assert both_sides.vertical == prefeature.vertical
  356:

from pathlib import Path
from michspc.fileio import geoid, pnezd, exports

from michspc.spc.units import METERS
from michspc.spc.vertical import NAVD88, NGVD29

src = pnezd.parse_lines(['101,47.1211,-88.5694,200.000,SWAP'])
def settings(src_model, out_model, *, source_datum=NAVD88, target_datum=NAVD88):
    return JobSettings(input_path=Path('in.csv'), output_directory=Path('.'), direction=Direction.VERTICAL_ONLY, source_zone=None, target_zone=None, input_unit=METERS, output_unit=METERS, longitude_convention=LongitudeConvention.NEGATIVE_WEST, vertical_mode=VerticalMode.VERTICAL, source_vertical_datum=source_datum, target_vertical_datum=target_datum, source_geoid_model=src_model, geoid_model=out_model)

    result = run(settings(sm, om), source=src)
    p = result.points[0]
    print(label, repr(p.output_elevation), repr(p.geoid_swap.shift_m if p.geoid_swap else None), repr(p.factors.geoid_height), repr(p.factors.ellipsoid_height))
    rows = exports.audit_rows(result)
    print('models', dict(zip(rows[0], rows[1])).get('Source geoid model'), dict(zip(rows[0], rows[1])).get('Geoid model'))
    print('clean fields', len(exports.clean_pnezd_rows(result)[0]), exports.clean_pnezd_rows(result)[0])

legacy = settings(None, geoid.GEOID18_MODEL, source_datum=NAVD88, target_datum=NGVD29)
explicit = settings(geoid.GEOID18_MODEL, None, source_datum=NAVD88, target_datum=NGVD29)
a = run(legacy, source=src); b = run(explicit, source=src)
pa, pb = a.points[0], b.points[0]
print('legacy==explicit', pa.output_elevation == pb.output_elevation, pa.factors == pb.factors, pa.vertical == pb.vertical, a.geoid_model, b.geoid_model)
print('legacy source column', 'Source geoid model' in exports.audit_columns(a))
print('explicit source column', 'Source geoid model' in exports.audit_columns(b))

report = build_report(swap_result)

+def test_a_geoid_refusal_names_the_side_the_grid_was_read_from():
+    """The GEOID_UNAVAILABLE warning must name the model actually consulted.
+
+    Per-side selection (#50) split one question into two, and this warning
+    kept naming ``settings.geoid_model`` - the OUTPUT side. On a
+    NAVD 88 -> NGVD 29 job the output side has no model at all (NGVD 29 has
+    none), so the factors run off the INPUT side and ``settings.geoid_model``
+    is None: naming it raised ``AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no
+    attribute 'name'`` and took the whole job down instead of warning about
+    one point. This is the GUI's DEFAULT state for that datum pair - the
+    output selector grays itself and emits None - so it needed only a point
+    off the tile to reach.
+
+    The point: 39.5 N is BELOW the GEOID18 CONUS grid #3's south edge of
+    40.0 N (``geoid.GEOID18_U3_GEOMETRY``), and VERTCON 3.0's CONUS grid
+    covers it, so the vertical shift succeeds and the geoid lookup is the
+    only thing that refuses - which is the order that reaches this branch.
+    """
+    settings = _swap_settings(
+        source_vertical_datum=NAVD88,
+        target_vertical_datum=NGVD29,
+        source_geoid_model=geoid.GEOID18_MODEL,
+        geoid_model=None,
+    )
+    assert geoid.GEOID18_U3_GEOMETRY.south_latitude == 40.0
+    assert settings.geoid_model is None
+
+    result = run(settings, source=pnezd.parse_lines(["1,39.5,-84.0,200.000,OFF"]))
+
+    messages = [
+        warning.message
+        for _row, warning in result.warnings
+        if warning.code is WarningCode.GEOID_UNAVAILABLE
+    ]
+    assert len(messages) == 1
+    # The INPUT side's model - the one `grid` was loaded from.
+    assert "no GEOID18 geoid height is available" in messages[0]
+    # And the point still converted: only the factors are unavailable.
+    assert result.points[0].factors.combined_factor is None
+    assert result.points[0].output_elevation is not None

@@ -64,80 +64,96 @@ class WarningCode(Enum):
 
     VERTICAL_SHIFT_UNAVAILABLE = "vertical-shift-unavailable"
     """A vertical job's point carried a usable elevation, but the VERTCON grid
     pair does not cover its position, so the elevation was NOT converted.
 
     Raised by michspc.job, like GEOID_UNAVAILABLE above. The horizontal
     coordinates are unaffected and stand; the point's output elevation is
     left blank rather than filled with the unshifted source-datum height,
     because an unconverted height printed in a Z column labelled with the
     target datum is exactly the ordinary-looking wrong number this program
     exists to refuse. VERTCON 3.0's CONUS grid covers all of Michigan
     (docs/PLAN-vertical-datums.md section 2.2), so this is only reachable for
     a point already far outside the zone extents - but "unreachable in
     practice" is not a reason to pass a height through silently when it is
     reached.
     """
 
     VERTICAL_SIGMA_UNAVAILABLE = "vertical-sigma-unavailable"
     """A vertical job's point was shifted, but no one-sigma uncertainty can be
     stated for the shift: the VERTCON error model interpolates below zero at
     the point's position, and a negative number is not an uncertainty
     (docs/DESIGN.md #36).
 
     Raised by michspc.job, like the two codes above. THE SHIFT ITSELF IS VALID
     AND UNAFFECTED - it comes from the separate transformation grid - so the
     point's elevation IS converted and written; only the sigma cell reads N/A.
 
     This code exists because DESIGN.md #41 recorded that the disclosure layer
     (WP-V7) must not assume a warning already flags the missing sigma: the
     reading carries its reason, but a reason on a frozen record reaches no
     surface on its own. Raising it here makes every surface - the job record's
     WARNINGS section, the audit CSV's warnings column, and the GUI warnings
     field - inherit the disclosure through the one warning pipeline they all
     already read, rather than each growing its own special case.
 
     NOT raised for an identity transformation: an identity carries no sigma
     because no model ran, which is a statement the job record's METHOD text
     makes ("no shift is applied"), not a condition worth warning about.
     """
 
+    GEOID_SWAP_UNAVAILABLE = "geoid-swap-unavailable"
+    """A geoid-to-geoid job's point carried a usable elevation, but one of the
+    two geoid tiles does not cover its position, so the elevation was NOT
+    re-derived under the output geoid model.
+
+    Raised by michspc.job, like the three codes above, and shaped exactly as
+    VERTICAL_SHIFT_UNAVAILABLE is: the horizontal coordinates are unaffected
+    and stand, and the point's output elevation is left blank rather than
+    filled with the input-model height, because a height stated against one
+    geoid model printed in a Z column whose job names another is the same
+    ordinary-looking wrong number. Both shipped tiles cover all of Michigan
+    with room to spare (docs/DESIGN.md section 3), so this is only reachable
+    for a point already far outside the state - but "unreachable in practice"
+    is not a reason to pass a height through silently when it is reached.
+    """
+
 
 @dataclass(frozen=True)
 class ConversionWarning:
     """Something the surveyor should look at, which is not a reason to refuse."""
 
     code: WarningCode
     message: str
 
 
 @dataclass(frozen=True)
 class PointConversion:
     """The full record of one point's conversion. Immutable.
 
     Every zone-derived field - the two zones, the four convergence and scale
     quantities, and the grid coordinates - is None on a record built by
     ``geodetic_position``: a vertical-only job whose input is geodetic
     involves no zone anywhere, and this program does not fabricate one. The
     geodetic pivot is the one thing such a record always carries.
     """
 
     source_zone: Zone | None
     target_zone: Zone | None
 
     frame: ReferenceFrame
     """The reference frame this position is expressed in.
 
     A geodetic position is meaningless without it (docs/DESIGN.md section 4:
     the pivot of every conversion is "a geodetic position tagged with its
     reference frame"). Carried on the record so the job record can state which
     frame the numbers were interpreted as, rather than leaving a reader to
     assume NAD 83 - the assumption that is a one-to-two metre error when it is
     wrong.
     """
 
     source_northing: float | None
     source_easting: float | None
     """Meters, in the source zone. None on a no-zone record."""
 
     latitude: float
     longitude: float

            problems.append((label, 'missing GEOID CHANGE'))

        """Calculate the default directory to use for temporary files.
        This routine should be called exactly once.
    
        We determine whether or not a candidate temp dir is usable by
        trying to create and write to a file in that directory.  If this
        is successful, the test file is deleted.  To prevent denial of
        service, the name of the test file must be randomized."""

                dir = _os.path.abspath(dir)

            for seq in range(100):
                name = next(namer)
                filename = _os.path.join(dir, name)
                try:
                    fd = _os.open(filename, _bin_openflags, 0o600)

                        try:
                            _os.write(fd, b'blat')
                        finally:
                            _os.close(fd)
                    finally:
                        _os.unlink(filename)
                    return dir

                    pass

                    # already exists on windows.

                        _os.access(dir, _os.W_OK)):

    def _get_default_tempdir(dirlist=None):
        """Calculate the default directory to use for temporary files.
        This routine should be called exactly once.
    
        We determine whether or not a candidate temp dir is usable by
        trying to create and write to a file in that directory.  If this
        is successful, the test file is deleted.  To prevent denial of
        service, the name of the test file must be randomized."""
    
        namer = _RandomNameSequence()
        if dirlist is None:
            dirlist = _candidate_tempdir_list()
    
        for dir in dirlist:
            if dir != _os.curdir:
                dir = _os.path.abspath(dir)
            # Try only a few names per directory.
            for seq in range(100):
                name = next(namer)
                filename = _os.path.join(dir, name)
                try:
                    fd = _os.open(filename, _bin_openflags, 0o600)
                    try:
                        try:
                            _os.write(fd, b'blat')
                        finally:
                            _os.close(fd)
                    finally:
                        _os.unlink(filename)
                    return dir
                except FileExistsError:
                    pass
                except PermissionError:
                    # This exception is thrown when a directory with the chosen name
                    # already exists on windows.
                    if (_os.name == 'nt' and _os.path.isdir(dir) and
                        _os.access(dir, _os.W_OK)):
                        continue
                    break   # no point trying more names in this directory
                except OSError:
                    break   # no point trying more names in this directory
>       raise FileNotFoundError(_errno.ENOENT,
                                "No usable temporary directory found in %s" %
                                dirlist)
E       FileNotFoundError: [Errno 2] No usable temporary directory found in ['C:\\Users\\marti\\AppData\\Local\\Temp', 'C:\\Users\\marti\\AppData\\Local\\Temp', 'C:\\Users\\marti\\AppData\\Local\\Temp', 'C:\\Windows\\Temp', 'c:\\temp', 'c:\\tmp', '\\temp', '\\tmp', 'C:\\claude-projects\\coord-convert']

    def _get_default_tempdir(dirlist=None):
        """Calculate the default directory to use for temporary files.
        This routine should be called exactly once.
    
        We determine whether or not a candidate temp dir is usable by
        trying to create and write to a file in that directory.  If this
        is successful, the test file is deleted.  To prevent denial of
        service, the name of the test file must be randomized."""
    
        namer = _RandomNameSequence()
        if dirlist is None:
            dirlist = _candidate_tempdir_list()
    
        for dir in dirlist:
            if dir != _os.curdir:
                dir = _os.path.abspath(dir)
            # Try only a few names per directory.
            for seq in range(100):
                name = next(namer)
                filename = _os.path.join(dir, name)
                try:
                    fd = _os.open(filename, _bin_openflags, 0o600)
                    try:
                        try:
                            _os.write(fd, b'blat')
                        finally:
                            _os.close(fd)
                    finally:
                        _os.unlink(filename)
                    return dir
                except FileExistsError:
                    pass
                except PermissionError:
                    # This exception is thrown when a directory with the chosen name
                    # already exists on windows.
                    if (_os.name == 'nt' and _os.path.isdir(dir) and
                        _os.access(dir, _os.W_OK)):
                        continue
                    break   # no point trying more names in this directory
                except OSError:
                    break   # no point trying more names in this directory
>       raise FileNotFoundError(_errno.ENOENT,
                                "No usable temporary directory found in %s" %
                                dirlist)

Instead, committed HEAD raises `AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'name'` and produces no result or archive.
